f(S^{l}({}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|^{\epsilon,l}\times[0,\alpha^{\epsilon}]))\subset{}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l+1}
. By the definition of the compact-open topology, this also holds for any
g
in a neighborhood
U
of
f
in
\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l})
.
Hence, we can cover
\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l})
by a family of opens
U_{i}
such that there exists
\alpha^{\epsilon}_{i}>0
satisfying
g\in U_{i}\Rightarrow g(S^{l}({}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|^{\epsilon,l}\times[0,\alpha^{\epsilon}_{i}]))\subset{}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l+1}
. By
Lemma

4.9
, we may assume that
K
is locally finite. But this implies that
\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l})
is metrizable, and hence, paracompact (see also
Remark

2.14
). In particular, one can find a partition of unity
(\phi_{i})
subordinated to the open cover
(U_{i})
. We can now define the following continuous map:

\displaystyle\alpha^{\epsilon}\colon\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l})

\displaystyle\to[0,1]

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{i}\phi_{i}(f)\alpha_{i}^{\epsilon}

Note that by construction, for all
f\in\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l})
, we have
\alpha^{\epsilon}(f)\leq\alpha_{i}^{\epsilon}
, for some
i
such that
f\in U_{i}
. In particular, we have

(12)

f(S^{l}({}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|^{\epsilon,l}\times[0,\alpha^{\epsilon}(f)]))\subset{}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l+1}

Now, to construct
\alpha
, we will use the family of functions
\alpha^{\epsilon}
, for
\epsilon=\frac{1}{2^{n}}
,
n\geq 1
. Consider the covering of
(0,1]
given by the family
I_{n}=(\frac{1}{2^{n+1}},\frac{1}{2^{n-2}})
,
n\geq 1
, and the family of closed intervals
J_{n}=\left[\frac{1}{2^{n}},\frac{1}{2^{n-1}}\right]
,
n\geq 1
. Pick a family of bump functions on
[0,1]
,
\psi_{n}
,
n\geq 1
satisfying :

•

\psi_{n}(s)\in[0,1]
, for all
s\in(0,1]
,

•

\psi_{n}(s)=1
if
s\in J_{n}
,

•

\psi_{n}(s)=0
if
s\not\in I_{n}
,

and define the continuous map:

\displaystyle\alpha\colon\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l})\times[0,1]

\displaystyle\to[0,1]

\displaystyle(f,s)

\displaystyle\mapsto s\prod_{n\geq 1}\left(1-\psi_{n}(s)(1-\alpha^{\frac{1}{2^{n}}}(f))\right)

Note that for
s\in[0,1]
, there is only a finite number of
n\geq 1
such that
\psi_{n}(s)\not=0
, which means that the above product only has a finite amount of non-trivial terms. We need to check that it satisfies both conditions of
Lemma

4.20
. Let
f\in\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l})
. The first part is clear, since
\alpha(f,s)=0
implies that either
s=0
, or that the product is
0
, which is not possible, since it only has a finite number of non-trivial terms which are all non-zero. For the second part, first note that if
s\in J_{n}

(13)

\alpha(f,s)\leq\alpha^{\frac{1}{2^{n}}}(f)

Now, if
t\in{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|
, then

•

either
\sum_{j=l+1}^{k}t_{q_{j}}=0
, but then
t\in{}|\Delta^{q_{0}<\dots<q_{l}}|
, and by
Lemma

4.18
, in this case
S^{l}(t,u)=t
for any
u\in[0,1]
. In particular, the expression in
Lemma

4.20
reduces to
f(t)
. Now,
f(t)
is of the form
(\sigma,(\xi_{0},\dots,\xi_{n}))
, but since
f
is stratum preserving, it must satisfy
\xi_{p}=0
for all
p>q_{l}
. Additionally, since
f
takes value in
{}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l}
, we have
\xi_{p}=0\Rightarrow\xi_{p^{\prime}}=0
for
p^{\prime}\geq p
and
p<q_{l}
. Now, since we already now that
\xi_{p^{\prime}}=0
for
p^{\prime}>q_{l}
, we trivially have the implication
\xi_{q_{l}}=0\Rightarrow\xi_{p^{\prime}}=0
for
p^{\prime}\geq q_{l}
. In particular
f(t)\in{}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l+1}
.

•

or
\sum_{j=l+1}^{k}t_{q_{j}}>0
. Then let
n
be such that
\sum_{j=l+1}^{k}t_{q_{j}}\in J_{n}=\left[\frac{1}{2^{n}},\frac{1}{2^{n-1}}\right]
. By definition,
t\in{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|^{\epsilon,l}
, for
\epsilon=\frac{1}{2^{n}}
. Combining equations (
13
) and (
12
), we get :

f(S^{l}(t,\alpha(f,\sum_{j=l+1}^{k}t_{q_{j}})))\in f(S^{l}({}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|^{\epsilon,l}\times[0,\alpha^{\epsilon}(f)]))\subset{}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l}

which concludes the proof.

∎

Proof of
Lemma

4.16
.

Using the map
\alpha\colon\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l})\times[0,1]\to[0,1]
from
Lemma

4.20
, we define the following homotopy:

\displaystyle H\colon\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l})\times[0,1]

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l})

\displaystyle(f,s)

\displaystyle\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}{}{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}&\to{}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l}\\
t=(t_{q_{0}},\dots,t_{q_{k}})&\mapsto f(S^{l}(t,(1-s)+s\alpha(f,\sum_{j={l+1}}^{k}t_{q_{j}})))\end{array}\right.

We first check that
H(f,s)
is a stratum preserving map. By
Lemma

4.18
,
f(S^{l}(t,u))
has the correct stratification, except maybe when
u=0
. In the latter case, one must have
s=1
and
\alpha(f,\sum_{j={l+1}}^{k}t_{q_{j}})=0
, but by
Lemma

4.20
, this is only possible when
\sum_{j={l+1}}^{k}t_{q_{j}}=0
. This corresponds to
t\in{}|\Delta^{[q_{0}<\dots<q_{l}]}|
, and by
Lemma

4.18
, in this case one has
S^{l}(t,u)=t
for all values of
u
. We conclude that
H(f,s)
is indeed a stratum preserving map. Furthermore, its image lies in
{}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l}
, by construction, which means that
H
is well-defined.
But now,
H_{0}
is the identity map, since
S^{l}(t,1)=t
for all
t\in{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|
. By the second part of
Lemma

4.20
, for all
f\in\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l})
, The image of
H(f,1)\colon\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to{}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l}
lies in
{}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l+1}
. This implies that
H_{1}
lands in
\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l+1})
. On the other hand, if
f\in\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l+1})
, then
H(f,s)
lies in
\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l+1})
for all
s\in[0,1]
. This shows that the inclusion
\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l+1})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}^{\text{red},l})
defines a homotopy equivalence (with inverse induced by
H_{1}
), which concludes the proof.
∎

4.5.
Realizations characterize weak-equivalences

We summarize the results of the previous subsections in the following theorem.

Theorem 4.21
.

Let
K
be a stratifed simplicial set,
\mathcal{I}
a regular flag and
b
a point in the interior of
{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|
. Then all maps in the following diagram are weak-equivalences:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textit{H\!\hskip 1.0pto}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}|K|_{N(P)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(K)|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|K|_{b}}

Corollary 4.22
.

Let
f\colon K\to L
be in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
. The following assertions are equivalent:

•

f
is a weak-equivalence in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
,

•

{}|f|_{N(P)}\colon{}|K|_{N(P)}\to{}|L|_{N(P)}
is a weak-equivalence in
\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}
,

•

{}{}|f|_{P}\colon{}{}|K|_{P}\to{}{}|L|_{P}
is a weak-equivalence in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
.

Furthermore, if
g\colon{}|K|_{N(P)}\to{}|L|_{N(P)}
is a map in
\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}
, then it is a weak-equivalence if and only if its image by the functor
\varphi_{P}\circ-\colon\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}\to\mathrm{Top}_{P}
is a weak-equivalence in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
.

Proof.

Let us first prove the second part. Let
g\colon{}|K|_{N(P)}\to{}|L|_{N(P)}
be a map in
\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}
. It is a weak-equivalence if and only if, for all regular flags
\mathcal{I}
, the maps induced by
g
,

\textit{H\!\hskip 1.0pto}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}|K|_{N(P)})\to\textit{H\!\hskip 1.0pto}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}|L|_{N(P)})

are weak-equivalences. But, by
Theorem

4.8
, this is equivalent to asking that the following maps are weak-equivalences:

\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P})\to\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|L|_{P})

This, in turn, is equivalent to
(\varphi_{P}\circ-)(g)
being a weak-equivalence in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
.

For the first part of the lemma, we know from
[
Dou21a
, Theorem 1.15]
that the functor
{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(-)|_{P}\colon\mathrm{sSet}_{P}\to\mathrm{Top}_{P}
is the left part of a Quillen-equivalence. In particular it characterizes weak-equivalences between cofibrant objects. Since all objects of
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
are cofibrant, this implies that
f
is a weak-equivalence if and only if
{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(f)|_{P}
is a weak-equivalence. Now consider the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(K)|_{N(P)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(L)|_{N(P)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|K|_{N(P)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|L|_{N(P)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(f)|_{N(P)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|\mathrm{l.v}_{P}|_{N(P)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|\mathrm{l.v}_{P}|_{N(P)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|f|_{N(P)}}

By
[
Dou21c
, Lemma A.3]
, we know that
\mathrm{l.v}_{P}\colon\mathrm{sd}_{P}(K)\to K
is a weak-equivalence for all
K\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
, and by
Proposition

4.6
, we also know that
{}|-|_{N(P)}
preserves weak-equivalences. This implies that the vertical arrows in the previous diagram are weak-equivalences. By two out of three, this means that
f
is a weak-equivalence if and only if
{}|f|_{N(P)}
is a weak-equivalence, which concludes the proof.
∎

Corollary 4.23
.

The functor
{}{}|C_{P}(-)|_{P}\colon\mathrm{Diag}_{P}\to\mathrm{Top}_{P}
characterizes all weak-equivalences.

Proof.

A map in
\mathrm{Diag}_{P}
,
f\colon F\to G
is a weak-equivalence if and only if, for all regular flags
\mathcal{I}
,
F(\mathcal{I})\to G(\mathcal{I})
is a weak-equivalence. On the other hand, the map
{}{}|C_{P}(f)|_{P}\colon{}{}|C_{P}(F)|_{P}\to{}{}|C_{P}(G)|_{P}
is a weak-equivalence in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
if and only if the map
\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|C_{P}(F)|_{P})\to\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|C_{P}(G)|_{P})
is a weak-equivalence for all
\mathcal{I}
. In particular, it is enough to show that for any
F\in\mathrm{Diag}_{P}
, the natural map
F(\mathcal{I})\to\mathrm{Sing}(\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|C_{P}(F)|_{P}))
is a weak-equivalence for all regular flags
\mathcal{I}
. Consider the following commutative diagram, where
b
is the barycenter of
{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|C_{P}(F)|_{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|F(\mathcal{I})|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textit{H\!\hskip 1.0pto}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}|C_{P}(F)|_{N(P)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|C_{P}(F)|_{b}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\,.}}

We need to prove that the top map is a weak-equivalence, but by
Theorem

4.21
, it is enough to show that the bottom map is a weak-equivalence. Now, note that
{}|C_{P}(F)|_{b}\cong{}|F(\mathcal{I})|\times\{b\}
, as can be computed directly from the definition of
C_{P}
. In particular, the bottom map is an isomorphism, which concludes the proof.
∎

Corollary 4.24
.

The functor
C_{P}\colon\mathrm{Diag}_{P}\to\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
characterizes all weak-equivalences.

Proof.

Let
f\colon F\to G
be a map in
\mathrm{Diag}_{P}
. By
Corollary

4.23
,
f
is a weak equivalence if and only if
{}{}|C_{P}(f)|_{P}
is a weak equivalence. But by
Corollary

4.22
, the latter is true if and only if
C_{P}(f)
is a weak-equivalence.
∎

5.
Equivalence of homotopy categories and applications

The goal of this section is to show that the adjoint functors

{}|-|_{\mathrm{Strat}}\colon s\mathrm{Strat}\leftrightarrow\mathrm{Strat}\colon\mathrm{Sing}_{\mathrm{Strat}},

as well as the fiberwise pairs
{}{}|-|_{P}\colon\mathrm{sSet}_{P}\leftrightarrow\mathrm{Top}_{P}\colon\mathrm{Sing}_{P}
descend to equivalences between the homotopy categories of stratified simplicial sets and stratified spaces.
We deduce from this that the naive homotopy theory of conically stratified spaces embeds fully faithfully in the homotopy theory of stratified spaces, as well as a simplicial approximation theorem.

5.1.
Equivalence between homotopy theories

In this subsection, we prove that the adjunctions
{}{}|-|_{P}\dashv\mathrm{Sing}_{P}
and
{}|-|_{\mathrm{Strat}}\dashv\mathrm{Sing}_{\mathrm{Strat}}
induce equivalences of homotopy categories. As neither is part of a Quillen adjunction, this is not to be understood in terms of derived functors in the sense of model categories. Instead, consider the homotopy categories as explicitly constructed by formally inverting the weak equivalences. All functors involved are shown to preserve weak equivalences and hence induce functors of the homotopy categories. The following theorem then states that these induced functors give equivalences of categories.

Theorem 5.1
.

The adjoint pairs
{}{}|-|_{P}\dashv\mathrm{Sing}_{P}
, and
{}|-|_{\mathrm{Strat}}\dashv\mathrm{Sing}_{\mathrm{Strat}}
induce well defined equivalences between homotopy categories,

\displaystyle{}{}|-|_{P}\colon\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{sSet}_{P}

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Top}_{P}\colon\mathrm{Sing}_{P}

\displaystyle{}|-|_{\mathrm{Strat}}\colon\mathrm{Ho}s\mathrm{Strat}

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Strat}\colon\mathrm{Sing}_{\mathrm{Strat}}.

We first prove that the functors of
Theorem

5.1
pass to the homotopy categories.

Lemma 5.2
.

The adjoint pairs
{}{}|-|_{P}\dashv\mathrm{Sing}_{P}
, and
{}|-|_{\mathrm{Strat}}\dashv\mathrm{Sing}_{\mathrm{Strat}}
induce well defined functors at the level of homotopy categories.

Proof.

By
Corollary

4.22
, the functor
{}{}|-|_{P}\colon\mathrm{sSet}_{P}\to\mathrm{Top}_{P}
preserves weak equivalences for all posets
P
. By construction of the model structures on
\mathrm{Strat}
and
s\mathrm{Strat}
, this implies that
{}|-|_{\mathrm{Strat}}
also preserves all weak-equivalences. Since the homotopy categories are nothing more than the localization of the categories at the classes of weak equivalences, this implies that
{}{}|-|_{P}
and
{}|-|_{\mathrm{Strat}}
both induce functors between homotopy categories. By
Theorem

3.16
,
\mathrm{Sing}_{P}\colon\mathrm{Top}_{P}\to\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
also preserve all weak-equivalences, and so the same is true for
\mathrm{Sing}_{\mathrm{Strat}}\colon\mathrm{Strat}\to s\mathrm{Strat}
, which means that they also induce well defined functors at the level of homotopy categories.
∎

To conclude the proof of
Theorem

5.1
, we need the following lemma.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
X
be a space stratified over
P
, then the co-unit of the adjunction
{}{}|-|_{P}\dashv\mathrm{Sing}_{P}
,

{}{}|\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(X)|_{P}\to X

is a weak-equivalence in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
.
Let
K
be a simplicial set stratified over
P
, then the unit of the adjunction
{}{}|-|_{P}\dashv\mathrm{Sing}_{P}
,

K\to\mathrm{Sing}_{P}({}{}|K|_{P})

is a weak-equivalence in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
.

This also holds for the unit and co-unit of the adjunction
{}|-|_{\mathrm{Strat}}\dashv\mathrm{Sing}_{\mathrm{Strat}}
.

Proof.

Consider first the adjunction
\mathrm{sd}_{P}\dashv\mathrm{Ex}_{P}
. For any simplicial set stratified over
P
,
K
, we have the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sd}_{P}(K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\,,}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sd}_{P}\mathrm{Ex}_{P}(K)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{l.v}_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sd}_{P}(\iota_{K})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{K}}

where the map
\epsilon_{K}
is the co-unit. Now, by
Proposition

2.62
,
\mathrm{l.v}_{P}
is a weak-equivalence, and
\mathrm{sd}_{P}
preserves weak-equivalences. By
Proposition

3.1
,
\iota_{K}
is a weak equivalence, which means that
\mathrm{sd}_{P}(\iota_{K})
is a weak-equivalence. By two out of three, this implies that
\epsilon_{K}\colon\mathrm{sd}_{P}\mathrm{Ex}_{P}(K)\to K
is a weak-equivalence.

Now let
X
be a space stratified over
P
, and consider the commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}\mathrm{Ex}_{P}\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(X)|_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(X)|_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\epsilon_{\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(X)}|_{P}}

By
Corollary

4.22
, the functor
{}{}|-|_{P}
preserves weak-equivalences, which means that the map
{}{}|\epsilon_{\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(X)}|_{P}
is a weak-equivalence. Furthermore, the co-unit of the adjunction
{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(-)|_{P}\dashv\mathrm{Ex}_{P}\mathrm{Sing}_{P}
, is also a weak-equivalence, since the adjunction is a Quillen-equivalence
[
Dou21a
, Theorem 1.15]
, and all objects in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
(resp.
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
) are fibrant (resp. cofibrant). This means that by two out of three the co-unit
{}{}|\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(X)|_{P}\to X
is a weak-equivalence.

Now, let
K
be a simplicial set stratified over
P
, and consider the following composition:

{}{}|K|_{P}\to{}{}|\mathrm{Sing}_{P}({}{}|K|_{P})|_{P}\to{}{}|K|_{P},

where the first map is the realization of the unit of the adjunction
{}{}|-|_{P}\dashv\mathrm{Sing}_{P}
, and the second map is the co-unit evaluated at
{}{}|K|_{P}
. The composition gives the identity, and we have proved that the second map is a weak-equivalence in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
, which means that the map
{}{}|K|_{P}\to{}{}|\mathrm{Sing}_{P}({}{}|K|_{P})|_{P}
is a weak-equivalence in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
, by two out of three. But since, by
Corollary

4.22
, the realization functor characterizes weak-equivalences, this means that the unit
K\to\mathrm{Sing}_{P}({}{}|K|_{P})
is a weak-equivalence in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
.

For the case of
{}|-|_{\mathrm{Strat}}\dashv\mathrm{Sing}_{\mathrm{Strat}}
, note that if
X
is a space stratified over
P
, then
{}|\mathrm{Sing}_{\mathrm{Strat}}(X)|_{\mathrm{Strat}}={}{}|\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(X)|_{P}
, by definition, which immediatly gives the proof.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

5.1
.

Consider the natural transformations
{}{}|\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(-)|_{P}\to 1_{\mathrm{Top}_{P}}
and
1_{\mathrm{sSet}_{P}}\to\mathrm{Sing}_{P}({}{}|-|_{P})
. By
Lemma

5.3
they take value in weak-equivalences. Since the functors
{}{}|-|_{P}
and
\mathrm{Sing}_{P}
pass to the homotopy categories, so do the natural transformations
{}{}|\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(-)|_{P}\to 1_{\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Top}_{P}}
and
1_{\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{sSet}_{P}}\to\mathrm{Sing}_{P}({}{}|-|_{P})
. Those now take value in isomorphisms, meaning that
({}{}|-|_{P},\mathrm{Sing}_{P})
gives an equivalence between the homotopy categories. The same argument gives that
({}|-|_{\mathrm{Strat}},\mathrm{Sing}_{\mathrm{Strat}})
induces an equivalence between the homotopy categories.
∎

Remark 5.4
.

Theorem

5.1
is only stated in terms of homotopy categories because that is all that is needed for the applications of
Sections

5.2
and
5.3
, but a much stronger version holds. Consider the simplicial localization defined by Dwyer and Kan in
[
DK80a
]
, in terms of hammocks.
Lemmas

5.2
and
5.3
give precisely the hypothesis needed to apply
[
DK80a
, Corollary 3.6]
. In particular, the functors
({}|-|_{\mathrm{Strat}},\mathrm{Sing}_{\mathrm{Strat}})
and
({}{}|-|_{P},\mathrm{Sing}_{P})
induce Dwyer-Kan equivalences between the simplicial localizations:

L^{H}s\mathrm{Strat}\leftrightarrow L^{H}\mathrm{Strat}\ \text{ and }\ L^{H}\mathrm{sSet}_{P}\leftrightarrow L^{H}\mathrm{Top}_{P}.

With the stronger result, one can say that the pairs of adjoint functors,
({}|-|_{\mathrm{Strat}},\mathrm{Sing}_{\mathrm{Strat}})
and
({}{}|-|_{P},\mathrm{Sing}_{P})
induce equivalences between the homotopy
theories
of stratified spaces and stratified simplicial sets. In particular, they induce equivalences between the underlying
\infty
-categories, which can be explicitly described as the Dwyer-Kan localizations.

5.2.
Embedding the classical stratified homotopy category

It follows from
Proposition

A.1
that there exists no model structure on
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
which is transported from
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
, along
\mathrm{Sing}_{P}\colon\mathrm{Top}_{P}\to\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
. Nevertheless, it turns out that stratified spaces with fibrant
\mathrm{Sing}_{P}
and realizations of stratified simplicial sets behave respectively much like fibrant and cofibrant objects of a model category.

Recollection 5.5
.

Recall that particularly nice stratified spaces, such as pseudo manifolds or more generally homotopically and conically stratified spaces, have the right lifting property with respect to realizations of admissible horn inclusions (see
Theorem

2.59
, and
[
Lur17
, Theorem A.6.4]
[
NL19
, Proposition 8.1.2.6]
). In other words, such spaces map to fibrant objects under
\mathrm{Sing}_{P}
.

Recall that for
X,Y\in\mathrm{Top}_{P}
,
[X,Y]_{P}
stands for the set of stratified homotopy classes of stratified maps between
X
and
Y
(
Definition

2.15
). similarly, if
K,L\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
,
[K,L]_{P}
stands for the set of stratified homotopy classes of stratified simplicial maps between
K
and
L
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{Top}_{P}
be a stratified space such that
\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(X)\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
is fibrant. Then, for any weak equivalence of stratified simplicial sets
f\colon K\to L
in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
, the induced map

[{}{}|L|_{P},X]_{P}\to[{}{}|K|_{P},X]_{P}

is a bijection.

Proof.

We have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[{}{}|L|_{P},X]_{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[{}{}|K|_{P},X]_{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[L,\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(X)]_{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[K,\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(X)]_{P}}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\,,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}

where the vertical maps are bijection, thanks to the fact that the adjunction
({}{}|-|_{P},\mathrm{Sing}_{P})
is simplicial
[
Dou21c
, Proposition 4.9]
.
Since
f\colon K\to L
is a weak equivalence and
\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(X)
is fibrant the lower horizontal is a bijection. Thus, by commutativity of the diagram, so is the upper horizontal, as was to be shown.
∎

As an immediate corollary of this lemma we obtain:

Theorem 5.7
.

Let
K\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
and
{X}\in\mathrm{Top}_{P}
such that
\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(X)
is a fibrant object of
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
. Then, the natural map

[{}{}|{K}|_{P},{X}]_{P}\to\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Top}_{P}({}{}|{K}|_{P},{X})

is a bijection.

Proof.

Consider the realization of the last vertex map
{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(K)|_{P}\xrightarrow{{}{}|\mathrm{l.v}_{P}|_{P}}{}{}|K|_{P}
. Since
\mathrm{l.v}_{P}
is a weak equivalence (
Proposition

2.62
) by
Corollary

4.22
so is its realization. Furthermore, it follows from
[
Dou21a
, Theorem 1.15]
, that
{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(K)|_{P}
is a cofibrant object in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
. Thus, it follows that
{}{}|\mathrm{l.v}_{P}|_{P}\colon{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(K)|_{P}\to{}{}|K|_{P}
defines a cofibrant replacement of
{}{}|K|_{P}
. We obtain a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[{}{}|{K}|_{P},{X}]_{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Top}_{P}({}{}|{K}|_{P},{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[{}{}|{\mathrm{sd}_{P}(K)}|_{P},{X}]_{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Top}_{P}({}{}|{\mathrm{sd}_{P}(K)}|_{P},{X}]_{P})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

Since,
{}{}|\mathrm{l.v}_{P}|_{P}
is a weak equivalence, the right vertical map is a bijection.
By
Lemma

5.6
, the same holds for the left vertical map. Since
{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(K)|_{P}
is cofibrant, and all object of
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
are fibrant, the bottom horizontal map is also a bijection. Hence, by commutativity of the diagram, the upper horizontal is a bijection, as was to be shown.
∎

By
5.5
we obtain the following immediate corollary of
Theorem

5.7
.

Corollary 5.8
.

Let
\mathrm{Con}_{P}\subset\mathrm{Top}_{P}
the full subcategory of conically
P
-stratified which are triangulable (stratum preserving homeomorphic to the realization of a stratified simplicial set). Denote by
\mathrm{Con}_{P}/{\simeq_{P}}
the category obtained by identifying stratum preserving homotopic maps. Then, the inclusion

\mathrm{Con}_{P}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Top}_{P}

induces a fully faithful embedding

\mathrm{Con}_{P}/{\simeq_{P}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Top}_{P}.

Remark 5.9
.

Theorems

5.7
and
5.8
also hold over varying posets, i.e. in
\mathrm{Strat}
. Indeed, it follows from the equivalence between the homotopy categories of
\mathrm{Strat}
and
\mathrm{sStrat}
which is
Theorem

5.1
, and the fibrancy property of conically stratified spaces, which also holds in
s\mathrm{Strat}
since fibrant objects of
s\mathrm{Strat}
are characterized fiberwise.

Remark 5.10
.

Note, that
Lemma

5.6
,
Theorem

5.7
and
Corollary

5.8
admit a strengthening in terms of simplicial categories. More precisely, if for
X,Y\in\mathrm{Top}_{P}
and
K,L\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
one replaces the set of homotopy classes of maps
[X,Y]_{P}
and
[K,L]_{P}
, by the simplicial mapping spaces, and the sets
\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Top}_{P}(X,Y)
and
\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{sSet}_{P}(K,L)
by derived mapping spaces, the statements remain true with analogous proofs. In practice, this means that when working with spaces satisfying the hypothesis of
Theorem

5.7
the classical mapping spaces has the correct homotopy type and there is no need to derive. This applies to triangulable conically stratified spaces as detailed in
Remark

5.11
.

Remark 5.11
.

The simplicial perspective described in
Remark

5.10
can be used to strengthen
Corollary

5.8
to a statement about infinity categories. Indeed,
\mathrm{Con}_{P}
, as a subcategory of
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
, inherits the structure of a simplicial category, while
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
itself is a simplicial model category. This means that the inclusion of the full simplicial sub-category
\mathrm{Con}_{P}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Top}_{P}
descends to a functor

\mathrm{Con}_{P}\hookrightarrow L^{H}_{\text{simp}}(\mathrm{Top}_{P})

Where
L^{H}_{\text{simp}}(\mathrm{Top}_{P})
is the diagonal hammock localization of a simplicial model category, described in
[
DK80b
, Prop. 4.8]
.
Corollary

5.8
then generalizes to the statement that the above map is a fully faithful embedding of simplicial categories. Combining this with the fact that
L^{H}_{\text{simp}}(\mathrm{Top}_{P})\simeq L^{H}(\mathrm{Top}_{P})
(
[
DK80b
, Prop. 4.8]
), this means that the "naive" infinity category of conically
P
-stratified and triangulable stratified spaces embeds fully faithfully in that of
P
-stratified spaces. The analogous statement for
\mathrm{Con}
and
\mathrm{Strat}
also holds by the same argument.

5.3.
A simplicial approximation theorem

Exposing
\mathrm{Ex}^{\infty}_{P}
as a fibrant replacement functor in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
allows one to study the homotopy category of
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
through actual maps in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
from some subdivision of the domain. Using
Theorem

5.1
, one can then transport those results to
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
and its homotopy category to obtain stratified versions of the classical simplicial approximation theorems.

Proposition 5.12
.

Let
K\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
be finite and
L\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
.
Then, for any morphism

\phi\colon K\to{L}
in the homotopy category
\mathrm{Ho}{\mathrm{sSet}}_{P}

there exists an
n\in\mathbb{N}
and a morphism
f\colon\mathrm{sd}^{n}_{P}(K)\to L
such that the diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sd}^{n}_{P}{{K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{l.v}_{P}^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}

commutes in
\mathrm{Ho}{\mathrm{sSet}_{P}}
.

Remark 5.13
.

Proposition

5.12
also holds as a relative version. To be more precise this involves the following replacements:
Replace
{K}
by a pair
{A}\hookrightarrow{K}
and
{L}
by some stratum preserving simplicial map
g:{A}\to{L}
. The role of
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
is then taken by the under category
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}^{A}
with the induced model structure from
[
Hir09
, Theorem 7.6.5]
. However, one needs to be mindful of the fact that, for the relative version,
\mathrm{sd}^{n}_{P}
maps into
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}^{\mathrm{sd}_{P}^{n}(A)}
. In particular, the final commutativity condition holds in the homotopy category of the latter. Aside from this, the proof is formally identical.

As an immediate corollary of this result (its relative version), and
Theorem

5.1
we obtain the following corollary, which we prove first.

Proposition 5.14
.

Suppose we are given a commutative diagram in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|A|_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|L|_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|K|_{P}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\,,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|g|_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|i|_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}

where
A,K
and
L
are stratified simplicial sets, with
A
and
K
finite,
i\colon A\hookrightarrow K
is an inclusion,
g\colon A\to L
some arbitrary map in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
and
\phi\colon{}{}|K|_{P}\to{}{}|L|_{P}
some map in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
.
Then, there exists an
n\in\mathbb{N}
and a map
\hat{f}\colon\mathrm{sd}^{n}_{P}(K)\to L\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
such that:

•

the following diagram commutes in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sd}_{P}^{n}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{l.v}^{n}_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{f}_{|\mathrm{sd}_{P}^{n}(A)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

•

the following diagram commutes in
\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Top}_{P}^{{}{}|\mathrm{sd}^{n}_{P}(A)|_{P}}

(14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}^{n}(K)|_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|K|_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|L|_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\mathrm{l.v}^{n}_{P}|_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\hat{f}|_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}

In particular, for
n\geq 1
, Diagram (
14
) can even be assumed to commute up to stratified homotopy relative to
{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}^{n}(A)|_{P}
, since then
{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}^{n}(i)|_{P}
is a cofibrant object in
\mathrm{Top}^{{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}^{n}(A)|_{P}}_{P}
.

Proof.

Note that, by subdividing the source once, we may without loss of generality assume that
i
is such that its realization is cofibrant in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
. As every object is fibrant, this means that morphisms in the homotopy category
\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Top}_{P}^{|A|}
from
{}{}|i|_{P}
to
{}{}|g|_{P}
agree with homotopy classes of maps in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}^{{}{}|A|_{P}}
(i.e. homotopy classes rel
{}{}|A|_{P}
).
Using this and (a relative version of)
Theorem

5.1
we obtain

[{}{}|i|_{P},{}{}|g|_{P}]^{{}{}|A|_{P}}_{P}=\mathrm{Ho}{\mathrm{Top}_{P}^{{}{}|A|_{P}}}({}{}|i|_{P},{}{}|g|_{P})\cong\mathrm{Ho}{\mathrm{sSet}_{P}}^{A}(i,g).

where the left hand side denotes relative stratum preserving homotopy classes and the right hand side bijection is given by realization.
Now, apply
Proposition

5.12
, to obtain the result.
∎

We now move on to the proof of
Proposition

5.12
. We are going to prove the nonrelative version. The relative proof is structurally almost identical. First, we need two equations involving subdivision and
\mathrm{Ex}_{P}
which are easily verified. Recall, that we denote
\iota^{n}:{L}\hookrightarrow{Ex}^{n}_{P}(L)
the natural inclusion induces by pulling back a simplex along
\mathrm{l.v}_{P}^{n}
.

Lemma 5.15
.

Denote by
\eta
and
\varepsilon
the unit and counit of
\mathrm{sd}^{n}_{P}\dashv\mathrm{Ex}^{n}_{P}
respectively. Then, the equations

\displaystyle\varepsilon\circ\mathrm{sd}_{P}^{n}(\iota^{n})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{l.v}_{P}^{n}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ex}^{n}_{P}(\mathrm{l.v}_{P}^{n})\circ\eta

\displaystyle=\iota^{n}

hold.

As an immediate corollary we obtain:

Corollary 5.16
.

Let
{K},{L}\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
. Then, the following diagram of bijections commutes:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ho}{\mathrm{sSet}_{P}}({K},{L})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ho}{\mathrm{sSet}_{P}}({K},\mathrm{Ex}^{n}_{P}(L))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ho}{\mathrm{sSet}_{P}}(\mathrm{sd}_{P}^{n}(K),{L})}

,

where the right diagonal is given by pre-composing with
\mathrm{l.v}^{n}
, the left diagonal by post-composing with
\iota^{n}
and the bottom horizontal is the adjunction map.

Proof.

Let
\phi\in\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{sSet}_{P}({K},{L})

Let
\hat{\phi}
be the adjoint morphism to
\iota^{n}\circ\phi
. It is given by
\varepsilon\circ\mathrm{sd}^{n}(\iota^{n}\circ\phi)
. By
Lemma

5.15
we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon\circ\mathrm{sd}^{n}(\iota^{n}\circ\phi)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{l.v}_{P}^{n}\circ\mathrm{sd}_{P}^{n}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\phi\circ\mathrm{l.v}_{P}^{n}.

In particular, this shows the required commutativity.
∎

We now have everything necessary available to derive
Proposition

5.12
.

Proof of
Proposition

5.12
.

By
Corollary

3.2
,
\iota^{\infty}:{L}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Ex}_{P}^{\infty}{L}
defines a fibrant replacement of
{L}
Now, consider the following commutative diagram:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sSet}_{P}({K},{L})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ho}{\mathrm{sSet}_{P}}({K},{L})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sSet}_{P}({K},\mathrm{Ex}^{\infty}_{P}(L))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ho}{\mathrm{sSet}_{P}({K},\mathrm{Ex}^{\infty}_{P}(L))}}

with the verticals given by postcomposition with
\iota^{\infty}
.
Since
\mathrm{Ex}_{P}^{\infty}
is fibrant, the lower horizontal is surjective.
As
{K}
is finite,
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}({K},\mathrm{Ex}_{P}^{\infty}(L))=\varinjlim\mathrm{sSet}_{P}(K,\mathrm{Ex}_{P}^{n}(L))
. In particular, for any
\phi\in\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{sSet}_{P}(K,L)
, we find some
f^{\prime}\colon{K}\to\mathrm{Ex}_{P}^{n}(L)
mapping to the same element as
\phi
in
\mathrm{Ho}{\mathrm{sSet}_{P}({K},\mathrm{Ex}_{P}^{\infty}{L})}
. As
\iota^{\infty}
is a weak equivalence and thus postcomposing with it gives a bijection in the homotopy category, we get that in particular
f^{\prime}=\iota^{n}\circ\phi
in
\mathrm{Ho}{\mathrm{sSet}}_{P}(K,\mathrm{Ex}_{P}^{n}(L))
.
Next, consider the following diagram, which is commutative by
Corollary

5.16
:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sSet}_{P}(K,{L})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ho}{\mathrm{sSet}_{P}}({K},{L})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sSet}_{P}(\mathrm{sd}_{P}^{n}(K),{L})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ho}{\mathrm{sSet}}_{P}(\mathrm{sd}_{P}^{n}(K),{L})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sSet}_{P}({K},\mathrm{Ex}^{n}_{P}(L))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ho}{\mathrm{sSet}_{P}}({K},\mathrm{Ex}^{n}_{P}(L))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

Bijections are marked by
\cong
.
We have shown that
f^{\prime}\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}(K,\mathrm{Ex}_{P}^{n}(L))
and
\phi\in\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{sSet}_{P}(K,L)
have the same image in
\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{sSet}_{P}(K,\mathrm{Ex}_{P}^{n}(L))
. But the commutativity of the diagram gives that the map
f\colon\mathrm{sd}^{n}_{P}(K)\to L\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
, adjoint to
f^{\prime}
, and
\phi\in\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{sSet}_{P}(K,L)
must have the same image in
\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{sSet}_{P}(\mathrm{sd}_{P}^{n}(K),L)
, which concludes the proof.
∎

6.
Simplicial Homotopy links and vertically stratified complexes

Consider again the following diagram of categories with weak equivalences:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Diag}_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sSet}_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Top}_{P}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\,.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|-|_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sing}_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{P}^{\mathrm{Top}}}

In
Section

4
we have seen that weak equivalences in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
are detected by (combinatorial) links and that (up to realization), those naturally have the same weak homotopy type as the homotopy links in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
. As a particular consequence, we obtained the fact that the realization functor
{}{}|-|_{P}
characterizes weak equivalences. The functor
D_{P}^{\mathrm{Top}}
characterizes weak equivalences by definition and it is the content of
Section

3
that
\mathrm{Sing}_{P}
characterizes weak equivalences. We have also shown (
Corollaries

4.24
and
4.23
) that
C_{P}
and thus
C_{P}^{\mathrm{Top}}={}{}|C_{P}(-)|_{P}
characterize weak-equivalences. To complete this picture, it remains to investigate the functor
D_{P}\colon\mathrm{sSet}_{P}\to\mathrm{Diag}_{P}
. Rephrasing this question in terms of links, we need to compare the combinatorial homotopy link
\mathrm{HoLink}
to the other notions of (homotopy) link, which we have already shown to agree (up to the Quillen equivalence
{}|-|\dashv\mathrm{Sing}
). There is an obvious natural comparison map

\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(K)=\mathrm{Map}(\Delta^{\mathcal{I}},K)\to\mathrm{Map}({}{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P},{}{}|K|_{P})=\mathrm{Sing}(\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P})

given by realization. The goal of this section is to prove the following theorem.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
K\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
. Then the natural inclusion

\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(K)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sing}(\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}))

is a weak equivalence of simplicial sets. Hence, equivalently, so is the adjoint map

{}|\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(K)|\to\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}).

As an immediate corollary of this result and
Corollary

4.22
, one obtains:

Corollary 6.2
.

The functor
D_{P}:\mathrm{sSet}_{P}\to\mathrm{Diag}_{P}
characterizes weak equivalences.

Proof.

By
Corollary

4.22
, a map
f\colon K\to L
in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
is a weak-equivalence if and only if
{}{}|f|_{P}\colon{}{}|K|_{P}\to{}{}|L|_{P}
is a weak-equivalence in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
. This implies that
f
is a weak-equivalence if and only if for all regular flags
\mathcal{I}
,
f
induces weak-equivalences
\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P})\to\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|L|_{P})
. By
Theorem

6.1
this is equivalent to asking that
f
induces weak-equivalences
D_{P}(K)(\mathcal{I})=\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(K)\to\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(L)=D_{P}(L)(\mathcal{I})
, which concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 6.3
.

Corollary

6.2
applies in particular to fibrant replacement maps
K\to K^{\mathrm{fib}}
(for example, the map
K\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Ex}^{\infty}_{P}(K)
). This means that the map
\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(K)\to\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(K^{\mathrm{fib}})
is a weak-equivalence, or in other words, that the naïve simplicial homotopy link and the fibrantly defined homotopy link coincide. This result provides insight as to why the model structure on
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
described in
[
Hen
]
and the one under studied in this paper coincide, even though they have very different descriptions. Philosophically, the former has a class of weak-equivalences defined from the naïve homotopy links
\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(K)
while the latter has a class of weak-equivalences defined from
\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(K^{\mathrm{fib}})
, and the weak-equivalence between those mapping spaces imply that the two model structures indeed have the same class of weak-equivalences. In fact, they coincide.

6.1.
Sketch of proof of
Theorem

6.1

We are going to prove
Theorem

6.1
through a comparison of homotopy groups using simplicial approximation style results. Note that using simplicial approximation to produce homotopies between simplicial maps leads to an uncommon type of cylinder object. To handle these cylinder objects, we define sd-homotopies.
Remark

6.8
, at the end of this subsection, provides some insight into sd-homotopies.

Definition 6.4
.

Let
S,S^{\prime}
be simplicial sets,
k\geq 0
, and
f,f^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S)\to S^{\prime}
be two simplicial maps. Then
f
and
f^{\prime}
are
sd-homotopic
, if there exists a simplicial map

H\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S\times\Delta^{1})\to S^{\prime}

such that
H
restricts on either end of the cylinder to
f
and
f^{\prime}
. Such a map is called an
sd-homotopy
. If
f
and
f^{\prime}
are pointed maps, a
pointed sd-homotopy
is instead a pointed map

H\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+})\to S^{\prime},

where
\wedge
stands for the smash product, and
\Delta^{1}_{+}
stands for the
1
-simplex with a freely adjoined base-point.

Now, let
K\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
be a stratified simplicial set,
\mathcal{I}\in R(P)
a regular flag and
f,f^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S)\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to K
two stratified simplicial maps. The maps
f
and
f^{\prime}
are called
(stratified) sd-homotopic
if there exists a stratum preserving simplicial map

H\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S\times\Delta^{1})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to K,

called a
(stratified) sd-homotopy in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
, such that
H
restricts on either end of the cylinder to
f
and
f^{\prime}
. If in addition
*\in S
and
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to K
are pointed, and
f
and
f^{\prime}
are pointed maps, then a
stratified, pointed sd-homotopy
is instead a pointed stratified map

H\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to K.

(Stratified) pointed sd-homotopies generate equivalence relations on the sets of pointed maps
\mathrm{sSet}^{*}(\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S),S^{\prime})
and
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}^{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}(\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S)\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}},K)
. We write
\sim_{*}
for both equivalence relations defined this way.

Remark 6.5
.

Let
f,f^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S)\to S^{\prime}
be two simplicial map, related by a simplicial homotopy
H\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S)\times\Delta^{1}\to S^{\prime}
. Note that there is a simplicial map

Q\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S\times\Delta^{1})\to\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S)\times\mathrm{sd}^{k}(\Delta^{1})\xrightarrow{1\times\mathrm{l.v}^{k}}\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S)\times\Delta^{1}.

Precomposing
H
with
Q
gives a sd-homtopy

H\circ Q\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S\times\Delta^{1})\to S^{\prime}

between
f
and
f^{\prime}
. In particular, if two maps are homotopic, they are also sd-homotopic. The analogous statement holds for stratified homotopies as well as in the pointed case.

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to K\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}^{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}
be an
\mathcal{I}
pointed stratified simplicial set. Consider
\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(K)
with the induced pointing.
Then, for each
n\geq 0
there are natural bijections

\displaystyle\varinjlim\mathrm{sSet}_{P}^{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}(\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S^{n})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}},K)/{\sim_{*}}

\displaystyle\cong\varinjlim\mathrm{sSet}^{*}(\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S^{n}),\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(K))/{\sim_{*}}

\displaystyle\cong\pi_{n}(\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(K))

where
S^{n}
is some triangulation of the
n
-sphere.
The first bijection is induced by the adjunction
-\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\dashv\mathrm{Map}(\Delta^{\mathcal{I}},-)
, and the second by composing with an inverse to
\mathrm{l.v}^{k}
(in the homotopy category).

Proof.

The first map is a bijection since, by construction, the simplicial adjunction

-\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\dashv\mathrm{Map}(\Delta^{\mathcal{I}},-)
preserve sd-homotopies. The second follows from the adjunction
\mathrm{sd}\dashv\mathrm{Ex}
, the fact that
\mathrm{Ex}^{\infty}
defines a fibrant replacement in
\mathrm{sSet}
, and that
S^{n}
is a finite simplicial set (see
Remark

6.8
for more details). Note, that the commutativity conditions needed to ensure that the map from the colimit is well defined were already checked in the proof of
Proposition

5.12
, specifically, in
Corollary

5.16
.
∎

We can now conclude the proof of
Theorem

6.1
.

proof of
Theorem

6.1
.

Let
K
be a stratified simplicial set, together with some pointing
\phi\colon\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to K
corresponding to a basepoint in
\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}({K})
. Consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varinjlim\mathrm{sSet}_{P}^{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}(\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S^{n})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}},K)/{\sim_{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[{}{}|S^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P},{}{}|K|_{P}\right]_{P}^{{}{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varinjlim\mathrm{sSet}^{*}(\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S^{n}),\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(K))/{\sim_{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[{}|S^{n}|,\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P})\right]^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{n}(\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(K))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{n}(\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}))\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\,,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

where the bottom horizontal map is induced by
\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}({K})\to\mathrm{Sing}(\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}({}{}|K|_{P}))
and the top horizontal
is given by first realizing and then precomposing with a homotopy inverse to
{}|\mathrm{l.v}^{k}|\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}
. Note that the top horizontal map is well-defined since (stratified) sd-homotopies realize to (stratified) homotopies, by the fact that
{}|\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S^{n}\times\Delta^{1})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|
is a cylinder for
{}|\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S^{n})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|
.

All the vertical maps are already known to be bijections. To finish the proof of
Theorem

6.1
, it suffices to show that the top horizontal map is a bijection. This is a consequence of
Proposition

6.7
. Indeed, the direct statement of
Proposition

6.7
gives surjectivity, while the homotopy statement shows that if two pointed maps
f,f^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S^{n})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to K
realize to homotopic maps, then they are related by a pointed sd-homotopy
\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}}(sd^{k}(S^{n})\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to K
. By
Remark

6.5
, this also means that
f\circ(\mathrm{l.v}^{k^{\prime}}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})\sim_{*}f^{\prime}\circ(\mathrm{l.v}^{k^{\prime}}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})
, which proves injectivity.
∎

Proposition 6.7
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{sSet}^{\star}
be a pointed finite simplicial set,
\mathcal{I}
a regular flag and
K\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}^{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}
a pointed stratified simplicial set. Then, for any pointed stratum preserving map
\phi\colon{}{}|S\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}\to{}{}|K|_{P}
and
k\gg 0
, there exists a pointed stratified simplicial map
f\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S)\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to K
such that

\phi\circ{}{}|\mathrm{l.v}^{k}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}|_{P}\simeq_{P}{}{}|f|_{P}\text{ rel }*\times{}{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}.

Conversely, if any two pointed stratified simplicial maps
f_{0},f_{1}:S\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to{K}
fulfill

{}{}|f_{0}|_{P}\simeq_{P}{}{}|f_{1}|_{P}\text{ rel }*\times{}{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P},

then, for
k\gg 0
, there exists a pointed sd-homotopy
H\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to K
between
f_{0}\circ(\mathrm{l.v}^{k}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})
and
f_{1}\circ(\mathrm{l.v}^{k}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})
.

Note, that in comparison to
Proposition

5.14
, the left hand side only subdivides in the nonstratified part of
S^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
. That such a more efficient subdivision suffices, is a consequence of the particularly simple shape of this stratified simplicial set. It is a special example of a vertically stratified object.
We will study those in details in
Section

6.2
.
This will serve a dual purpose. First off, we use these objects to obtain a proof of
Proposition

6.7
in
Section

6.3
. Secondly, they give a simple and convenient model for the homotopy category of stratified spaces (see
Theorem

6.26
).

Remark 6.8
.

Simplicial approximation theorems, such as
Proposition

5.14
, allow one to produce a simplical maps
f\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S)\to S^{\prime}
from the data of a continuous map
\phi\colon{}|S|\to{}|S^{\prime}|
. The two maps will then be related by a (topological) homotopy

H\colon{}|\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S)|\times[0,1]\to{}|S^{\prime}|

relating
{}|f|
and
\phi\circ{}|\mathrm{l.v}^{k}|
.
In its relative version, one can in addition assume that if
\phi
was already simplicial on some subobject
A\subset S
, i.e.
\phi_{|{}|A|}={}|g|
, with
g\colon A\to S^{\prime}
, then
f
can be chosen such that
f_{\mathrm{sd}^{k}(A)}=g\circ\mathrm{l.v}^{k}
, and the homotopy
H
can then be taken relative to
{}|\mathrm{sd}^{k}(A)|
.

One common use of the relative statement is when one has a pair of simplicial maps
f,f^{\prime}\colon S\to S^{\prime}
whose realization happen to be homotopic, through some map
H^{\prime}\colon{}|S\times\Delta^{1}|\cong{}|S|\times[0,1]\to{}|S^{\prime}|
. Through relative simplicial approximations, one gets a map

H\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S\times\Delta^{1})\to S^{\prime},

which restricts to
f\circ\mathrm{l.v}^{k}
and
f^{\prime}\circ\mathrm{l.v}^{k}
on either side of the cylinder
\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S\times\Delta^{1})
. Now, note that
H
is not an elementary simplicial homotopy (see
Definition

2.22
), but instead an sd-homtopy. In particular, unless one is given an appropriate simplicial map
\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}}(S)\times\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime\prime}}(\Delta^{1})\to\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S\times\Delta^{1})
, one can not deduce that, after enough subdivisions, the maps
f\circ\mathrm{l.v}^{k}
and
f^{\prime}\circ\mathrm{l.v}^{k}
become simplicially homotopic in the usual sense. It does not seem unreasonable to assume that such a map can generally be exposed, in fact in the unordered simplicial complex case, such a result follows from the material presented in
[
Spa89
, Chapter 3, Section 4]
.
We have no need for such a result in the following proof, however, and will work with the alternative cylinder instead.
Of course,
f
and
f^{\prime}
are still equal in the homotopy category, and if
S^{\prime}
is fibrant, this is enough to show that they must be related through a simplicial homotopy.

One way to derive these type of simplicial approximation results, which we already employed in the proof of
Proposition

5.12
, is by leveraging the
\mathrm{Ex}^{\infty}
functor. Let us again illustrate this for homotopies. Let
f,f^{\prime}\colon S\to S^{\prime}
be two maps who become equal in the homotopy category (this is equivalent to asking that they realize to homotopic maps).
\iota\colon S^{\prime}\to\mathrm{Ex}^{\infty}(S^{\prime})
be the usual inclusion. Then, since
\mathrm{Ex}^{\infty}
is a fibrant replacement functor,
f\circ\iota
and
f^{\prime}\circ\iota
must be related through a simplicial homotopy
\hat{H}\colon S\times\Delta^{1}\to\mathrm{Ex}^{\infty}(S^{\prime})
. Now, if
S
is finite, the map
\hat{H}
must factor through
\mathrm{Ex}^{n}(S^{\prime})
for some
n\geq 0
. Using the adjunction
\mathrm{sd}^{n}\dashv\mathrm{Ex}^{n}
, we get an sd-homtopy

H\colon\mathrm{sd}^{n}(S\times\Delta^{1})\to S^{\prime}

which restricts on either side of the cylinder
\mathrm{sd}^{n}(S\times\Delta^{1})
to
f\circ\mathrm{l.v}^{n}
and
f^{\prime}\circ\mathrm{l.v}^{n}
. Again, note that, a priori, this is not enough to conclude that after enough subdivisions,
f\circ\mathrm{l.v}^{n}
and
f^{\prime}\circ\mathrm{l.v}^{n}
become simplicially homotopic in the usual sense.

6.2.
Vertically stratified objects

Throughout this subsection, if
\sigma\colon\Delta^{n}\to S
is a possibly degenerate simplex,
\widehat{\sigma}
stands for the unique non-degenerate simplex of
S
, of which
\sigma
is a degeneracy.

Definition 6.9
.

A
P
-labelled simplicial set
is the data of

•

a simplicial set
S
;

•

a labelling map,
\lambda_{S}\colon S_{\mathrm{n.d.}}\to N(P)_{\mathrm{n.d.}}
,

such that, for any
\sigma\subset\tau
in
S
,
\lambda_{S}(\tau)\subset\lambda_{S}(\sigma)
. A
label preserving map

f\colon(S,\lambda_{S})\to(S^{\prime},\lambda_{S^{\prime}})
is a simplicial map
f\colon S\to S^{\prime}
, such that for all
\sigma\in S_{\mathrm{n.d.}}
,
\lambda_{S}(\sigma)\subset\lambda_{S^{\prime}}(\widehat{f(\sigma)})
.
We denote by
P\text{-}\mathrm{sSet}
the category of
P
-labelled simplicial sets with label preserving maps.

Example 6.10
.

Let
K\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
be a stratified simplicial set. Consider the (non-stratified) simplicial set
\mathrm{sd}(K)
. Define a labelling map,
\lambda\colon\mathrm{sd}(K)_{\mathrm{n.d.}}\to N(P)_{\mathrm{n.d.}}
as follows. If
(\mu,\sigma)
is a vertex in
\mathrm{sd}(K)
, with
\mu\subset\Delta^{n}
and
\sigma\colon\Delta^{n}\to K
, let
\lambda(\mu,\sigma)=\widehat{\varphi_{K}\circ\sigma\circ\mu}
. For higher dimensional simplices, set
\lambda((\mu_{0},\dots,\mu_{k}),\sigma)=\lambda(\mu_{0},\sigma)
. Then
(\mathrm{sd}(K),\lambda)
is a
P
-labelled simplicial set, and for any stratum preserving simplicial map
f\colon K\to L
,
\mathrm{sd}(f)\colon(\mathrm{sd}(K),\lambda)\to(\mathrm{sd}(L),\lambda)
is a label-preserving map.

Definition 6.11
.

Let
(S,\lambda_{S})
be a
P
-labelled simplicial set. Its
verticalization
is the inclusion of stratified simplicial sets
V(S,\lambda_{S})\hookrightarrow S\times N(P)
, where
V(S,\lambda_{S})
is defined as the following subset:

V(S,\lambda_{S})=\bigcup\limits_{\sigma\colon\Delta^{n}\to S\textnormal{ , n.d.}}\mathrm{Im}(\sigma)\times\lambda_{S}(\sigma)\subset S\times N(P)\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\,,}

where the union is taken over all non-degenerate simplices of
S
.
If
f\colon(S,\lambda_{S})\to(S^{\prime},\lambda_{S^{\prime}})
is a label preserving map, then the map
f\times 1_{N(P)}
restricts to a stratified map
V(f)\colon V(S,\lambda_{S})\to V(S^{\prime},\lambda_{S^{\prime}})
and
V
defines a functor
P\text{-}\mathrm{sSet}\to\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
in this fashion.

Next, let us pay some more attention to the stratified simplicials sets which lie in the essential image of the verticalization functor.

Definition 6.12
.

A
pre-verticalization
on a stratified simplicial set
K
is the data of

•

a simplicial set
\bar{K}
;

•

a monomorphism in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
,
K\hookrightarrow\bar{K}\times N(P)
.

A
vertical map
between stratified simplicial sets equipped with pre-verticalizations
K\subset\bar{K}\times N(P)
and
L\subset\bar{L}\times N(P)
is a stratum preserving map
f\colon K\to L
such that there exists a simplicial map
\bar{f}\colon\bar{K}\to\bar{L}
making the following diagram commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{K}\times N(P)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{L}\times N(P)\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\,.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{f}\times 1_{N(P)}}

Definition 6.13
.

A
vertically stratified simplicial set
is a stratified simplicial set,
K
, equipped with a pre-verticalization,
K\hookrightarrow\bar{K}\times N(P)
, which is isomorphic to the verticalization of a
P
-labelled simplicial set through some vertical map:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(S,\lambda_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{K}\times N(P)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\times N(P)\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\,.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Remark 6.14
.

Note that for a stratified simplicial set, having a pre-verticalization is not enough to be a vertically stratified simplicial set. All stratified simplicial sets admit a tautological pre-verticalization,
K\to K\times N(P)
. However, we are only interested in those pre-verticalizations that come from taking the verticalizations of
P
-labelled simplicial sets. Furthermore, when considering vertical maps between those, one need not keep track of the associated simplicial map
\bar{f}\colon\bar{K}\to\bar{L}
since in those cases, if such a map exists, it is unique. To see this, observe that for a vertically stratified simplicial set
K\to\bar{K}\times N(P)
, the composition
K\to\bar{K}\times N(P)\to\bar{K}
must be surjective, leaving at most one choice for
\bar{f}
(see also the fully faithfulness part of
Proposition

B.3
).

Example 6.15
.

Let
K\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
be a stratified simplicial set, and
(\mathrm{sd}(K),\lambda)
the
P
-labelled simplicial set from
Example

6.10
. Then
V(\mathrm{sd}(K),\lambda)=\mathrm{sd}_{P}(K)\subset\mathrm{sd}(K)\times N(P)
. Furthermore, if
f\colon K\to L
is a stratified map, then
\mathrm{sd}_{P}(f)=V(\mathrm{sd}(f))
is a vertical map. See
Fig.

7
.

[1]
[1]
[0]
[0]
[0<1]
[0<1]

Figure 7.
The figure
8
as a stratified simplicial set over
P=\{0<1\}
, its subdivision as a
P
-labelled simplicial set, and the associated vertically stratified simplicial set,
\mathrm{sd}_{P}(K)
.

Remark 6.16
.

Vertically stratified simplicial sets can be characterized in another way. They are precisely the images of cofibrant objects in
\mathrm{Diag}_{P}
under the functor
C_{P}\colon\mathrm{Diag}_{P}\to\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
. Indeed, one can go from a
P
-labelled simplicial set,
(S,\lambda_{S})
, to a (cofibrant) diagram,
F
, by setting
F(\mathcal{I})=\{\sigma\in S\ |\ \Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\subset\lambda_{S}(\widehat{\sigma})\}
, for regular flags
\mathcal{I}
,. Conversely, given a cofibrant diagram
F
, set
S=\cup_{\mathcal{I}}F(\mathcal{I})
, and
\lambda_{S}(\sigma)=\max\{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\ |\ \sigma\in F(\mathcal{I})\}
. To see why this labelling map is well-defined, recall from
Proposition

2.42
that cofibrant objects in
\mathrm{Diag}_{P}
are precisely the diagrams,
F
, such that:

•

For all
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{I}
, the map
F(\mathcal{I})\to F(\mathcal{I}^{\prime})
is a monomorphism.

•

If
\mathcal{I}_{0}\subset\mathcal{I}_{1},\mathcal{I}_{1}^{\prime}
, then
F(\mathcal{I}_{0})\supset F(\mathcal{I}_{1})\cap F(\mathcal{I}_{1}^{\prime})\not=\emptyset
if and only if there exist
\mathcal{I}_{2}
such that
\mathcal{I}_{1},\mathcal{I}_{1}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{I}_{2}
, and in this case,
F(\mathcal{I}_{1})\cap F(\mathcal{I}_{1}^{\prime})=F(\mathcal{I}_{2})
, for the smallest such
\mathcal{I}_{2}
.

One then checks that the verticalization process corresponds to applying
C_{P}
to the corresponding diagram.

This relation between vertically stratified simplicial sets and diagrams fits into a larger picture, which is explored in more details in
Appendix

B
, more specifically
Proposition

B.2
.

Proposition 6.17
.

Let
f\colon S\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to L
in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
be a stratum preserving simplicial map between vertically stratified simplicial sets. There exists a stratum preserving homotopy,
H\colon(S\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}})\times\Delta^{1}\to L
such that
H_{0}=f
and
H_{1}
is a vertical map. Furthermore, if
f
is already vertical on
A\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\subset S\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
, then the homotopy
H
, can be taken relative to
A\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
.

Proof.

We give an explicit definition of the homotopy for the case
S=\Delta^{n}
which is natural in
n
and hence extends to the general case.
Note that
f
factors through
S^{\prime}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\subset L
, where
L=V(\bar{L},\lambda_{\bar{L}})
, and
S^{\prime}\subset\bar{L}
is spanned by the simplices
\sigma
such that
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\subset\lambda_{\bar{L}}(\sigma)
.
Hence, we may without loss of generality assume
L
to be of the form
S^{\prime}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
for some
S^{\prime}\in\mathrm{sSet}
.
In particular, as
f
is stratum preserving, it is uniquely determined by the map
\hat{f}:\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to S^{\prime}
obtained by composing with the projection to
S^{\prime}
, and we may equivalently show the existence of a simplicial map (natural in
\Delta^{n}
and
S^{\prime}
)
\hat{H}\colon\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\times\Delta^{1}\to S^{\prime}
such that
\hat{H}_{0}=\hat{f}
and
\hat{H}_{1}
factors through
\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to\Delta^{n}
.
Any
k
-simplex in
\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\times\Delta^{1}
is given by the data of a flag

(x_{0},p_{0})\leq\dots\leq(x_{k},p_{k})

with
x_{i}\in[n]
and
p_{i}\in\mathcal{I}
, together with some
l\in[k+1]
indicating whether the vertices
(x_{i},p_{i})
project to
0
or
1\in\Delta^{1}
. More explicitely,
l
stands for the
k
-simplex of
\Delta^{1}
,
[0,\dots,0,1,\dots 1]
, where the first
1
appears at the
l
-th position. Note that a simplex
([(x_{0},p_{0}),\dots(x_{k},p_{k})],l)
lies in the sub-object
\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\times\{0\}
(resp.
\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\times\{1\}
) if and only if
l=k+1
(resp.
l=0
).
Using this, consider the (not stratum preserving) simplicial map

\displaystyle R^{n}\colon\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\times\Delta^{1}

\displaystyle\to\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}

\displaystyle([(x_{0},p_{0}),\dots(x_{k},p_{k})],l)

\displaystyle\mapsto[(x_{0},p_{0}),\dots,(x_{l},p_{l}),(x_{l+1},p_{m}),\dots,(x_{k},p_{m})],

where
p_{m}
is the maximum of
\mathcal{I}
. It is immediate from the definition, that
R^{n}
is natural in
n
and furthermore, that
R^{n}_{0}=1
and
R^{n}_{1}
factors through
\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to\Delta^{n}
.
Now, finally define
\hat{H}
as the composition

\hat{H}:\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\times\Delta^{1}\xrightarrow{R^{n}}\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\xrightarrow{\hat{f}}S^{\prime},

which fufills the requirements by the respective properties of
R^{n}
, and is natural in n. Finally, for the relative statement, note that if
\hat{f}
already factors through
\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to\Delta^{n}
, i.e.
f
is vertical on
\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
, then we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\times\Delta^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{f}}

In other words,
\hat{H}
factors through
\pi_{\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}:\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\times\Delta^{1}\to\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
and thus is a constant homotopy, which shows the relative statement.
∎

The theory of
P
-labelled and of vertically stratified simplicial sets has a topological counterpart, which we briefly introduce here.

Definition 6.18
.

A
P
-labelled CW-complex
is the data of

•

a CW-complex,
T
;

•

a labelling map,
\lambda_{T}\colon\{\text{cells of $T$}\}\to N(P)_{\mathrm{n.d.}}
.

Such that, for any pair of cells
e_{\alpha},e_{\beta}
such that
e_{\alpha}\cap\overline{e_{\beta}}\not=\emptyset
,
\lambda_{T}(e_{\beta})\subset\lambda_{T}(e_{\alpha})
.
A
label preserving map

f\colon(T,\lambda_{T})\to({T^{\prime}},\lambda_{{T^{\prime}}})
is a continuous map
f\colon T\to{T^{\prime}}
, such that for any cells
e_{\alpha}\in T,e_{\beta}\in{T^{\prime}}
such that
f(e_{\alpha})\cap e_{\beta}\not=\emptyset
, one has
\lambda_{T}(e_{\alpha})\subset\lambda_{T^{\prime}}(e_{\beta})
. We denote the category of
P
-labeled CW-complexes by
P\text{-}\mathrm{CW}
.

Example 6.19
.

Given a
P
-labelled simplicial set
(T,\lambda_{T})
, its realization admits the structure of a
P
-labelled CW-complex with a cell for each non-degenerate simplex, which is given the same label as the simplex.

As for
P
-labelled simplicial sets, we are interested in the verticalization of a
P
-labelled CW-complex.

Definition 6.20
.

Let
(T,\lambda_{T})
be a
P
-labelled CW-complex. Its
verticalization
is the inclusion
V(T,\lambda_{T})\hookrightarrow T\times{}{}|N(P)|_{P}
, where
V(T,\lambda_{T})
is the following subset:

V(T,\lambda_{T})=\bigcup\limits_{e_{\alpha}\in\{\text{cells of $T$}\}}e_{\alpha}\times{}{}|\lambda_{T}(e_{\alpha})|_{P}\subset T\times{}{}|N(P)|_{P}.

Extending to morphisms in the obvious way, this construction defines a functor
V\colon P\text{-}\mathrm{CW}\to\mathrm{Top}_{P}
, which factors through
V\colon P\text{-}\mathrm{CW}\to\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}
.

Again, it can be useful to study the objects in the essential image of the verticalization functor. These lead to a particularly convenient class of stratified spaces which admit a cell decomposition similar to classical CW-complexes (see
Remark

6.22
). We give a brief outlook into the resulting theory here, without going too much into detail. The definitions require a definition of a
pre-verticalization
and
vertical map
, which we omit here, since they are entirely analogous to the simplicial definition in
Definition

6.12
, replacing
N(P)
by
{}{}|N(P)|_{P}
.

Definition 6.21
.

A
vertically stratified CW-space
is a stratified space equipped with a pre-verticalization, which is vertically isomorphic to the verticalization of a
P
-labeled CW-complex. When such an isomorphism is fixed we speak of a
vertically stratified CW-complex
. We will consider those objects in the categories
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
and
\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}
.

Remark 6.22
.

Vertically stratified CW-spaces can also be characterized explicitly through the existence of a cell decomposition. Though, in this case, one has to be careful to also impose verticality conditions on the gluing of cells. Since the cells are of shape
B^{n}\times{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{N(P)}
, for
n\geq 0
, and regular flags
\mathcal{I}
, and are glued along their boundaries
S^{n-1}\times{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{N(P)}
, this implies that vertically stratified CW-spaces are actual cell complexes in
\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}
(and in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
). In particular, they are cofibrant objects.

Example 6.23
.

Following
Examples

6.15
and
6.19
, given a stratified simplicial set
K\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
, we have a
P
-labelled CW-complex
({}|\mathrm{sd}(K)|,{}|\lambda|)
. Its verticalization gives a vertically stratified CW-structure on
{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(K)|_{P}
:

{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(K)|_{P}\hookrightarrow{}|\mathrm{sd}(K)|\times{}{}|N(P)|_{P}.

See
Proposition

B.3
for a complete picture of the relationship between labelled objects, vertical objects and diagrams indexed over
R(P)
.

Proposition 6.24
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a map in
\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}
between vertically stratified CW-spaces,
X\cong V(\bar{X},\lambda_{\bar{X}})
and
Y\cong V(\bar{Y},\lambda_{\bar{Y}})
. Then
f
is strongly stratified homotopic to a vertical map. Furthermore, if
\bar{A}\subset\bar{X}
is a subcomplex equipped with the induced labelling, then the homotopy can be taken relative to
A=V(\bar{A},\lambda_{\bar{A}})\subset X
. The analogous statement also holds for maps and homotopies in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
.

Proof.

Here, we give a proof using the cellular structure of vertically stratified CW-spaces. A proof going through abstract homotopy theory is given in
Appendix

B
.
We first prove the statement for maps in
\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}
by the usual induction on skeletons argument. Let us write
X^{n}=V(\bar{A}\cup\bar{X}^{n},\lambda_{\bar{X}})
, for
n\geq-1
. We will construct a sequence of maps,
g_{i}\colon X\to Y
,
i\geq-1
, with
g_{-1}=f
, and
g_{n}
vertical on
X^{n}
, and
g_{n}
and
g_{n-1}
homotopic relative to
X^{n-1}
, for
n\geq 0
, through some homotopy in
\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}
,
H_{n}
. By subdividing the interval, one can then concatenate the homotopies
H_{n}
, to a homotopy
H\colon X\times[0,1)\to Y
. By construction, the map
g\colon x\mapsto\lim_{t\to 1}H(x,t)
is well defined, vertical and extends
H
to
X\times[0,1]
. Hence,
g
is as required in the statement of the proposition.

Now, let
n\geq 0
, and assume
g_{n-1}
has been constructed. Let
e_{\alpha}
be some
n
-cell of
X
of shape
B^{n}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
and define the homotopy

\displaystyle H^{\prime}_{n,\alpha}\colon B^{n}\times{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{N(P)}\times[0,1]

\displaystyle\to Y\subset{\bar{Y}}\times{}|N(P)|_{N(P)}

\displaystyle((x,\xi),s)

\displaystyle\mapsto(pr_{\bar{Y}}(g_{n-1}(x,s(1,0,\dots,0)+(1-s)\xi)),\xi)

Assembling the
H^{\prime}_{n,\alpha}
gives a homotopy on the
n
-skeleton
H^{\prime}_{n}\colon X^{n}\times[0,1]\to Y
, which is constant on the
n-1
skeleton, by the induction hypothesis. But now, by
Remark

6.22
, the inclusion
X^{n}\to X
is a cofibration in
\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}
, and thus has the homotopy extension property. This means that
H^{\prime}_{n}
extends to the desired homotopy
H_{n}\colon X\times[0,1]\to Y
, between
g_{n-1}
and some map
g_{n}
with the required properties, which concludes the proof for
\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}
.

For maps in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
, first note that, as noticed in Remark
6.22
,
X
and
Y
are cofibrant objects, in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
, and in fact come from cofibrant objects in
\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}
. Since all objects in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
and
\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}
are fibrant, and since
\varphi_{P}\circ-\colon\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}\to\mathrm{Top}_{P}
is a Quillen equivalence between the two model categories, this implies that we have canonical bijections

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Top}_{P}}(X,Y)/{\simeq_{P}}

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Top}_{P}}(X,Y)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}}(X,Y)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}}(X,Y)/{\simeq_{N(P)}}.

Where
\simeq_{P}
and
\simeq_{N(P)}
stand for the homotopy relations in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
and
\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}
respectively. In particular, this implies that there must exist
f^{\prime}\colon X\to Y
a map in
\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}
homotopic to
f
as a map in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
.
For the relative statement, consider the relative categories
\mathrm{Top}_{P}^{A}
, and
\mathrm{Top}^{A}_{N(P)}
. The Quillen equivalence between
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
and
\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}
passes to the relative categories, and the above argument then gives the existence of a map in
\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}^{A}
,
f^{\prime}\colon X\to Y
, homotopic to
f
as a map of
\mathrm{Top}_{P}^{A}
. In other words, it is homotopic to
f
relative to
A
.
We then conclude the proof by applying the first part of the proposition to
f^{\prime}
.
∎

Definition 6.25
.

Two vertical maps between vertically stratified CW-spaces,
f,g\colon X\to Y
, are said to be
vertically homotopic
if there exists a vertical map
H\colon X\times[0,1]\to Y
which is a homotopy between
f
and
g
. Write
f\simeq_{V}g
when
f
and
g
are vertically homotopic, and let
\mathrm{VCW}_{P}/{\simeq_{V}}
be the category whose objects are vertically stratified CW-spaces and whose set of morphisms between
X
and
Y
is the set of vertical maps quotiented by the equivalence relation
\simeq_{V}
.

Now, as a corollary of
Proposition

6.24
, we obtain that the homotopy category
\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Top}_{P}
may equivalently be described as the homotopy category of vertically stratified CW-spaces
\mathrm{VCW}_{P}/{\simeq_{V}}
, and vertical maps and homotopies. The comparison is given by the forgetful functor, sending a vertically stratified CW-space
X\hookrightarrow\bar{X}\times{}{}|N(P)|_{P}
to the stratified space
X
.

Theorem 6.26
.

the forgetful functor induces an equivalence of categories

\mathrm{VCW}_{P}/{\simeq_{V}}\cong\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Top}_{P}.

Proof.

By classical results, we know that
\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Top}_{P}\cong\mathrm{Top}_{P}^{\mathrm{cof}}/{\simeq_{P}}
. Furthermore, for all
X\in\mathrm{Top}_{P}
,
X
is weakly equivalent to
{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(X))|_{P}
, which admits the structure of a vertically stratified CW-complex by
Example

6.23
. Since stratified spaces admitting the structure of a vertically stratified CW-complexes are cofibrant objects, when
X
is also a cofibrant object it is in fact homotopy equivalent to the vertically stratified CW-complex
{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(X))|_{P}
.
Thus, one can restrict from the subcategory of cofibrant objects, to the subcategory of vertically stratified CW-spaces (while still retaining all stratum preserving maps and homotopies). But then, by
Proposition

6.24
, it is enough to consider only vertical maps, and, from the relative case, we see that it is also enough to consider only vertical homotopies between vertical maps, which concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 6.27
.

Theorem

6.26
implies that vertically stratified CW-complexes and vertical maps (and equivalently labeled CW-complex with label preserving maps, see
Appendix

B
, specifically
Corollary

B.6
) are a model for the homotopy category of stratified spaces. This is particularly convenient for a few reasons.

•

As illustrated in the proof of
Proposition

6.24
, vertically stratified CW-complexes allow for inductive arguments on their skeletons, giving access to reasonably elementary and topological proofs.

•

Vertically stratified CW complexes allow for a nice interpretation of the homotopy links, and the stratified homotopy groups. Indeed, let
X\cong V(\bar{X},\lambda_{\bar{X}})\subset\bar{X}\times{}{}|N(P)|_{P}
be a vertically stratified CW-complex, let
\mathcal{I}
be a regular flag, and let
\bar{X}_{\mathcal{I}}\subset\bar{X}
be the subcomplex containing all cells
e_{\alpha}\subset\bar{X}
, such that
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\subset\lambda_{\bar{X}}(e_{\alpha})
. Then, there is a homotopy equivalence

\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(X)\simeq\bar{X}_{\mathcal{I}}

where the map from
\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(X)
to
\bar{X}_{\mathcal{I}}
is given by evaluating at the
0
-th vertex, then projecting to
\bar{X}
, while its homotopy inverse is the map sending a point
x\in\bar{X}_{\mathcal{I}}
to the composition
\{x\}\times{}{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}\hookrightarrow\bar{X}_{\mathcal{I}}\times{}{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}\hookrightarrow X
. In particular, the
p
-stratum of
X
is homotopy equivalent to the subobject
\bar{X}_{p}\subset\bar{X}
, and for all regular flags
\mathcal{I}=\{p_{0}<\dots<p_{n}\}
, the above equivalence can be rewritten as

\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(X)\simeq\bar{X}_{p_{0}}\cap\dots\cap\bar{X}_{p_{n}}.

In particular, for a vertically stratified CW-complex, its strata - and homotopy links - can be interpreted as subobjects - and intersections of those subobjects - of the associated
P
-labelled CW-complex.
Even more, a pointing
x
of
\bar{X}
in a cell with label
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
gives a stratified pointing
\phi\colon{}{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}\to X
(and all pointing of
X
come from restrictions of such pointings, up to homotopy). Thus, the stratified homotopy groups of
[
Dou21b
]
associated to the pointing
\phi
are nothing more than the data of the homotopy groups of the
\bar{X}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}
, for
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{I}
, with respect to the pointing
x
.

•

Given a vertically stratified CW-complex
X\cong V(\bar{X},\lambda_{\bar{X}})
its underlying (non-stratified) homotopy type is that of the CW-complex
\bar{X}
. Indeed, to see this, consider the diagram of spaces associated to
V(X,\lambda_{X})
,
F\colon\mathcal{I}\mapsto\bar{X}_{\mathcal{I}}
defined as in the previous bulletpoint (in the appendix this construction is denoted
U^{\mathrm{Top}}
, see
Proposition

B.2
for more details).
Then, the underlying space of
X\cong V(\bar{X},\lambda_{\bar{X}})
is a simplicial model for the homotopy colimit of
F
(compare for ex
[
Hir09
, Sec. 18.1]
). At the same time
\bar{X}
is the regular colimit of
F
. Furthermore, it follows immediately from the construction of
F
, that it is a cofibrant diagram in the projective model structure, on the category of functors from
R(P)^{op}
to
\mathrm{Top}

Proposition

B.2
.
In particular (see for example
[
Dug08
, Prop 9.11]
), we have weak equivalences of topological spaces:

X=\operatornamewithlimits{hocolim}F\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatornamewithlimits{colim}F=\bar{X}.

The analogous result holds for vertically stratified simplicial sets.

6.3.
One last approximation theorem

We now move on to the proof of
Proposition

6.7
.
We first need a technical lemma on how to concatenate sd-homotopies.

Lemma 6.28
.

Let
S,S^{\prime}
be simplicials sets and
Let
H\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S\times\Delta^{1})\to S^{\prime}
as well as
H^{\prime}:\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S\times\Delta^{1})\to S^{\prime}
be sd-homotopies between simplicial maps
f
and
g
, and
g
and
h
respectively. Then, there exists a concatenated sd-homotopy
H^{\prime\prime}\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k+2}(S\times\Delta^{1})
from
f\circ\mathrm{l.v}^{2}
to
h\circ\mathrm{l.v}^{2}
. The analogous result for pointed and stratified sd-homotopies also holds.

Proof.

We prove the non-pointed non-stratified case. The other cases work completely analogously.
First consider a gluing

H\cup H^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S\times\Delta^{1})\cup_{\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S)}\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S\times\Delta^{1})\to S^{\prime}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\,,}

gluing
H
to
H^{\prime}
along
g
.
The left hand side of this map is equivalently given by

\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S\times(\Delta^{1}\cup_{1,0}\Delta^{1}))=\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S\times(\Delta^{1}\cup_{1,0}\Delta^{1}))\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\,.}

Note, that we may naturally identify

\mathrm{sd}^{2}(\Delta^{1})=\Delta^{1}\cup_{1,1}\Delta^{1}\cup_{0,0}\Delta^{1}\cup_{1,1}\cup\Delta^{1}.

Using this identification, one obtains a map

c\colon\mathrm{sd}^{2}(\Delta^{1})\to(\Delta^{1}\cup_{1,0}\Delta^{1})

given by collapsing the second and the fourth interval of
\mathrm{sd}^{2}(\Delta^{1})
to a point. In particular, this map maps left endpoint to left endpoints and right endpoints to right endpoints. From
c
, we in turn now obtain the composition

c^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{sd}^{2}(S\times\Delta^{1})\to\mathrm{sd}^{2}(S)\times\mathrm{sd}^{2}(\Delta^{1})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{l.v}^{2}\times c}S\times(\Delta^{1}\cup_{1,0}\Delta^{1})

mapping the left boundary of the subdivided cylinder
\mathrm{sd}^{2}(S\times\Delta^{1})
to the left boundary of the glued cylinder
S\times(\Delta^{1}\cup_{1,0}\Delta^{1})
and analogously for the right boundary. Precomposing
H^{\prime}\cup H^{\prime}
, with
\mathrm{sd}^{k}(c^{\prime})
produces an sd-homotopy
H^{\prime\prime}
as required.
∎

Next, we show the following.

Lemma 6.29
.

Let
S\in\mathrm{sSet}
be a finite simplicial set with simplicial subset
S_{0}\subset S
. Further, let
K\cong V(\bar{K},\lambda_{\bar{K}})\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
be a vertically stratified simplicial set, and
\mathcal{I}
a regular flag of
P
. Assume that we are given a map in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
,
\phi\colon{}{}|S\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}\to{}{}|K|_{P}
and a vertical map in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}

g\colon S_{0}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to K
, such that

\phi|_{{}{}|{S_{0}}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}}={}{}|g|_{P}.

Then for
k\gg 0
there exists a vertical, simplicial map
f\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S)\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to{K}
, such that

f|_{\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S_{0})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}=g\circ(\mathrm{l.v}^{k}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})

and such that
{}{}|f|_{P}
is stratum preserving homotopic to
\phi\circ{}{}|\mathrm{l.v}^{k}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}|_{P}
relative to
{}{}|\mathrm{sd}^{k}({S_{0}})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}
.

Proof.

By
Proposition

6.24
, there exists a vertical map
h\colon{}{}|S\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}\to{}{}|K|_{P}
, that is stratified homotopic to
\phi
relative to
{}{}|{S_{0}}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}
.
The image of
\bar{h}
must be contained in the cells with labels containing
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
. We write
K_{\mathcal{I}}
for the corresponding subsimplicial set of
\bar{K}
. The situation can be summed up by the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|{S_{0}}|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|K_{\mathcal{I}}|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|S|}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|\bar{g}|}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{h}}

Now, by the non-stratified, relative version of
Proposition

5.12
(see also
Remark

5.13
), there exists some
