k\geq 0
and some simplicial map
h^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S)\to K_{\mathcal{I}}
such that the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sd}^{k}({S_{0}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{\mathcal{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{g}\circ\mathrm{l.v}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{\prime}}

and such that
{}|h^{\prime}|
is homotopic to
\bar{h}\circ{}|\mathrm{l.v}^{k}|
relative to
\mathrm{sd}^{k}({S_{0}})
. But now the composition

\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S)\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\xrightarrow{h^{\prime}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}}K_{\mathcal{I}}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\hookrightarrow{K}

is the desired map.
∎

We may now combine the previous two lemmas, to obtain the following proposition.

Proposition 6.30
.

Let
S
be a finite pointed simplicial set and
\mathcal{I}
some regular flag in
P
. Then, for any
S_{0}\subset S
a simplicial subset containing the base point, there exists a pointed vertical simplicial map

\ell\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S)\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to\mathrm{sd}_{P}(S\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}})

which restricts to a pointed vertical simplicial map

\ell_{0}\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}({S_{0}})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to\mathrm{sd}_{P}({S_{0}}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}})

such that the diagram

(15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S_{0})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sd}_{P}(S_{0}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{0}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ell_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{l.v}_{P}}

commutes up to pointed sd-homotopy.

Before we begin with the proof, let us quickly remark on the somewhat specific wording of
Proposition

6.30
.

Remark 6.31
.

Proposition

6.30
is at the heart of the proof of
Proposition

6.7
. We effectively use it in two ways. First, for the existence part of
Proposition

6.7
, one uses the case
S_{0}=S
guaranteeing the existence of a global
l
with certain commutativity properties. Then, for the uniqueness part (up to sd-homotopy) one considers the case where
S
is a cylinder with boundary
S_{0}
. In that case, it is crucial that the restriction of the global map
\ell
to
\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S_{0})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
has its image in
\mathrm{sd}_{P}(S_{0}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}})
, however, the commutativity property is then only needed for that restriction.

Proof of
Proposition

6.30
.

To simplify notation, we omit all exponents from last vertex maps. Note, that
{}{}|\mathrm{l.v}_{P}|_{P}\colon{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(S\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}})|_{P}\to{}{}|S\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}
is a weak equivalence between cofibrant, fibrant objects with respect to the model structure on
\mathrm{Top}_{P}^{{}{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}}
. In particular, it has an inverse, up to stratum preserving pointed homotopy equivalence. Denote these by
\gamma_{S}
and
\gamma_{S_{0}}
respectively. By naturality of
\mathrm{l.v}_{P}
the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|{S_{0}}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}{({S_{0}}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})|_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|S\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(S\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}})|_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{S_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{S}}

is commutative in
\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Top}_{P}^{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}
. Now, since all objects involved are cofibrant, the diagram is commutative up to pointed stratum preserving homotopy. Now, first apply
Lemma

6.29
to
\gamma_{S_{0}}
with the role of the simplicial subset taken by the basepoint. For some
k^{\prime}\geq 0
, we obtain a pointed vertical simplicial map
\ell^{1}_{S_{0}}\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}}({S_{0}})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to\mathrm{sd}_{P}({S_{0}}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}})
such that the following solid diagram commutes up to pointed, stratum preserving homotopy. To reduce the overload of notation, we omit the indices
P
for stratified realizations.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}}({S_{0}})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}}({S_{0}})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|{S_{0}}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}{({S_{0}}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}}(S)\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}}(S)\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|S\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}{(S\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})|.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}|}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|\ell^{1}_{{S_{0}}}|}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}|}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{S_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|\ell^{2}_{S}|}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}|}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\gamma}_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}|\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}|}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{S}}

Now, in this diagram the vertical left solid arrow is a cofibration in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}^{{}{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}}
and all objects are cofibrant fibrant. Since the outer solid arrow diagram is commutative up to stratum preserving pointed homotopy, the homotopy extension property of cofibrations against fibrant objects gives the existence of a dashed map
\tilde{\gamma}_{S}
, making the square commute on the nose, and making the lower right triangle commute up to stratum preserving homotopy.
Now, apply
Lemma

6.29
to
\tilde{\gamma}_{S}
and
\ell^{1}_{S_{0}}
to obtain a pointed vertical simplicial map
\ell^{2}_{S}
in the dotted part of the diagram, for
k^{\prime\prime}
sufficently large. Again, the most outer part of the complete diagram commutes on the nose (in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
) and the lower left triangle commutes up to stratum preserving pointed homotopy.

Now
\ell^{2}_{S}
is not yet the map we are looking for, since we still need to check that (
15
) commutes up to pointed sd-homotopy.
Write
\ell^{2}_{S_{0}}=\ell^{1}_{S_{0}}\circ(\mathrm{l.v}^{k^{\prime\prime}}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})
, for the restriction of
\ell^{2}_{S}
to
\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}}({S_{0}})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
.
We want to construct a simplicial homotopy between
\mathrm{l.v}_{P}\circ\ell^{2}_{S_{0}}
and
\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}
, but to apply
Lemma

6.29
we need two vertical simplicial maps. What we will do instead is, first replace
\mathrm{l.v}_{P}\circ\ell^{2}_{S_{0}}
by a vertical map, and then, exhibit a sd-homotopy from the latter to
\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}
. Finally we will concatenate the two sd-homotopies using
Lemma

6.28
.
By
Proposition

6.17
there exists a simplicial pointed stratum preserving homotopy

H_{1}\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}}(S_{0})\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to S_{0}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}

from
\mathrm{l.v}_{P}\circ\ell^{2}_{S_{0}}
to a basepoint preserving vertical simplicial map
f\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}}(S_{0})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to S_{0}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
. Note that, by construction,
\mathrm{l.v}_{P}\circ\ell^{2}_{S_{0}}
realizes to a map that is stratum preserving pointed homotopic to the realization of
\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}
, and so it must also be true of
f
. More specifically, there must exist a pointed stratified homotopy

H^{\prime}\colon{}{}|\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}}(S_{0})\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}\to{}{}|S_{0}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}

Between
{}{}|f|_{P}
and
{}|\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}|_{N(P)}
. Since
f
and
\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}
are both pointed vertical maps, they define a vertical map

f\vee(\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}}(S_{0})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\vee\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}}(S_{0})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to S_{0}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}

We can now apply the relative version of
Lemma

6.29

to
H^{\prime}
and
f\vee(\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})
to get a simplicial map

H_{2}\colon sd^{k^{\prime\prime\prime}}(\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}}(S_{0})\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to S_{0}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}

Now consider boundary preserving maps

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}\colon

\displaystyle\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}}(S_{0}\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+})\to\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}}(S_{0})\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+}

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}=\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime\prime\prime}}(\alpha_{1})\colon

\displaystyle\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}+k^{\prime\prime\prime}}(S_{0}\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+})\to\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime\prime\prime}}(\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}}(S_{0})\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+}).

The compositions
H_{1}^{\prime}=H_{1}\circ(\alpha_{1}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})\circ(\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})
and
H_{2}^{\prime}=H_{2}\circ(\alpha\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})
give pointed sd-homotopies respectively between
\mathrm{l.v}_{P}\circ\ell^{2}_{S_{0}}\circ(\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})
and
f\circ(\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})
and between
f\circ(\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})
and
\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}
. Using
Lemma

6.28
we may concatenate these sd-homotopies
to a sd-homotopy

H\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}+k^{\prime\prime\prime}+2}(S_{0}\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to S_{0}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}},

between
\mathrm{l.v}_{P}\circ\ell^{2}_{S_{0}}\circ(\mathrm{l.v}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}})
and
\mathrm{l.v}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
.

Finally, we take
k=k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}+k^{\prime\prime\prime}+2
, and
\ell
to be the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S)\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sd}^{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}}(S)\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sd}_{P}(S\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ell^{2}_{S}}

As, we have just proven, the restriction of this map to
\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S_{0})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
satisfies the homotopy commutativity of (
15
).
∎

We now have all the necessary technical results to prove
Proposition

6.7
.

proof of
Proposition

6.7
.

We first prove the direct statement. Note that a pointing
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to K
canonically lifts along
\mathrm{l.v}_{P}
to a pointing
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to\mathrm{sd}_{P}(K)
. The lift is given by
the composition

\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to\mathrm{sd}_{P}(\Delta^{\mathcal{I}})\to\mathrm{sd}_{P}(K)

where the first map is specified by sending the the maximal non-degenate simplex
\mu
of
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
to
[(\mu,p_{0}),\dots,(\mu,p_{n})]
,
for
\mathcal{I}=[p_{0}<\cdots<p_{n}]
.
We then have a diagram in the pointed category

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|S\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(K)|_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|{K}|_{P}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\mathrm{l.v}_{P}|_{P}}

Note, that
{}{}|\mathrm{l.v}_{P}|_{P}
is a weak equivalence between fibrant objects in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}^{{}{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}}
, and thus,
\phi
admits a lift,

\hat{\phi}
, making the above diagram commutative up to stratum preserving pointed homotopy. Now, apply the relative version of
Lemma

6.29
to
\hat{\phi}
, to produce a pointed map
\hat{f}\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S)\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to\mathrm{sd}_{P}(K)
. The map
f=\mathrm{l.v}_{P}\circ\hat{f}\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S)\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to K
is then the desired map. Indeed, we have the following pointed stratum preserving homotopies:

{}{}|f|_{P}={}{}|\mathrm{l.v}_{P}\circ\hat{f}|_{P}\simeq_{P}{}{}|\mathrm{l.v}_{P}|_{P}\circ\hat{\phi}\circ{}{}|\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}|_{P}\simeq_{P}\phi\circ{}{}|\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}|_{P}.

Now, for the homotopy statement, take
{S^{\prime}_{0}}:=S\vee S\hookrightarrow S\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+}=:S^{\prime}
, and set

g^{\prime}=f_{0}\cup_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}f_{1}:{S_{0}}^{\prime}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to{K}

and let
H^{\prime}\colon{}{}|S^{\prime}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}\to{}{}|K|_{P}
be the stratified pointed homotopy between
{}{}|f_{0}|_{P}
and
{}{}|f_{1}|_{P}
. Next, consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}({S^{\prime}_{0}}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}})|_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}{{K}}|_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(S^{\prime}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}})|_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|{K}|_{P}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\,.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(g^{\prime})|_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\mathrm{l.v}_{P}|_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\prime}\circ{}{}|\mathrm{l.v}_{P}|_{P}}

Since the left hand vertical is a cofibration in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
and the right hand vertical is a weak equivalence between fibrant objects, there must exist some lift up to homotopy,
\phi^{\prime}
, making the upper left triangle commute on the nose and the lower right triangle commute up to stratified homotopy relative to
{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}({S^{\prime}_{0}}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}})|_{P}
. Now, precompose this diagram with
\ell
from
Proposition

6.30
, for
k\gg 0
to obtain the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S^{\prime}_{0})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}({S^{\prime}_{0}}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}})|_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}{{K}}|_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\mathrm{sd}^{k}(S^{\prime})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(S^{\prime}\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}})|_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\ell_{0}|_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\mathrm{sd}_{P}(g^{\prime})|_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\ell|_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{\prime}}

Set
\phi=\phi^{\prime}\circ{}{}|\ell|_{P}
and
g=\mathrm{sd}_{P}(g^{\prime})\circ\ell_{0}
. Now, apply
Lemma

6.29
to
\phi
and
g
. For
k^{\prime}\gg 0
, we obtain a simplicial map

H^{1}\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k+k^{\prime}}(S^{\prime})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to\mathrm{sd}_{P}(K)

whose restriction to
\mathrm{sd}^{k+k^{\prime}}(S_{0}^{\prime})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
is given by
\mathrm{sd}_{P}(g^{\prime})\circ\ell_{0}\circ(\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})
. By naturality of
\mathrm{l.v}_{P}
one has
\mathrm{l.v}_{P}\circ\mathrm{sd}_{P}(g^{\prime})=g^{\prime}\circ\mathrm{l.v}_{P}
. In particular,
\mathrm{l.v}_{P}\circ H^{1}\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k+k^{\prime}}(S^{\prime})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to K
restricts on the boundary to
g^{\prime}\circ\mathrm{l.v}_{P}\circ\ell_{0}\circ(\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})
.
By
Proposition

6.30
, this map is sd-homotopic to
g^{\prime}\circ(\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})
through a map of the form

H^{2}\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k+k^{\prime}}({S^{\prime}_{0}}\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to K

Now, the two sd-homotopies
\mathrm{l.v}_{P}\circ H^{1}
and
H^{2}
can be concatenated. More explicitely, consider the inclusion
i_{1}\colon S^{\prime}_{0}\to S^{\prime}
and
i_{2}\colon S^{\prime}_{0}=S^{\prime}_{0}\wedge\{0\}_{+}\to S^{\prime}_{0}\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+}
. These maps induce inclusions

\displaystyle i^{\prime}_{1}\colon

\displaystyle\mathrm{sd}^{k+k^{\prime}}(S^{\prime}_{0})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{sd}^{k+k^{\prime}}(S^{\prime})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}

\displaystyle i^{\prime}_{2}\colon

\displaystyle\mathrm{sd}^{k+k^{\prime}}(S^{\prime}_{0})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{sd}^{k+k^{\prime}}(S^{\prime}_{0}\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}

The restrictions
\mathrm{l.v}_{P}\circ H^{1}\circ i^{\prime}_{1}
and
H^{2}\circ i^{\prime}_{2}
are both equal to
g^{\prime}\circ\mathrm{l.v}_{P}\circ\ell_{0}\circ(\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})
, by construction. In particular,
\mathrm{l.v}_{P}\circ H^{1}
and
H^{2}
can be glued along
\mathrm{sd}^{k+k^{\prime}}(S^{\prime}_{0})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
to obtain a map

H^{3}\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k+k^{\prime}}(S^{\prime}\cup_{{S^{\prime}_{0}}}(S^{\prime}_{0}\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+}))\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to{K},

which restricts to
g^{\prime}\circ(\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})=(f_{0}\cup_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}f_{1})\circ(\mathrm{l.v}\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})
on the boundary. Now to turn this map into an sd-homotopy, consider the following composition:

\alpha\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k+k^{\prime}+2}(S\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+})\to\mathrm{sd}^{k+k^{\prime}}(S\wedge\mathrm{sd}^{2}(\Delta^{1}_{+}))\to\mathrm{sd}^{k+k^{\prime}}(S^{\prime}\cup_{S^{\prime}_{0}}(S^{\prime}_{0}\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+})),

where the left hand map comes from the natural map
\mathrm{sd}^{2}(S\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+})\to S\wedge\mathrm{sd}^{2}(\Delta^{1}_{+})
and the right hand map is the one suggested by
Fig.

8
. To construct the latter explicitely, it is enough to note that there is an isomorphism

S^{\prime}\cup_{{S^{\prime}_{0}}}(S^{\prime}_{0}\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+})\cong S\wedge(\Delta^{1}\cup_{1,0}\Delta^{1}\cup_{1,1}\Delta^{1})_{+},

and a boundary preserving map
\mathrm{sd}^{2}(\Delta^{1})\to\Delta^{1}\cup_{1,0}\Delta^{1}\cup_{1,1}\Delta^{1}
. Now, the desired pointed sd-homotopy is given by the composition

H=H^{3}\circ(\alpha\times 1_{\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}})\colon\mathrm{sd}^{k+k^{\prime}+2}(S\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+})\times\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\to K.

∎

Figure 8.
From left to right, schematic pictures of the simplicial sets,
S
,
S^{\prime}_{0}
,
S^{\prime}
,

, the union
S^{\prime}\cup_{S^{\prime}_{0}}(S^{\prime}_{0}\wedge\Delta^{1}_{+})
and the smash product
S\wedge\mathrm{sd}^{2}(\Delta^{1}_{+})
. Pointing are indicated by large dots, and arrows indicate direction of homotopies.

Appendix A
Incompatible criteria for model structures over
\mathrm{Top}_{P}

The goal of this section is to prove the following proposition, and its corrolary.

Proposition A.1
.

For
P
a non-discrete poset, there exists no model structure on
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
satisfying all of the following properties:

(1)

Realizations of monomorphisms are cofibrations.

(2)

Stratified homotopy equivalences are weak equivalences.

(3)

For a weak equivalence, the induced map between classical homotopy links is also
a weak-equivalence.

Remark A.2
.

In fact, we will prove a slightly stronger version of
Proposition

A.1
, where condition
3
is replaced by the following:

(3’)

For some fixed flag,
\mathcal{I}=\{p<q\}
, the homotopy link functor
\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}
sends weak equivalences to weak equivalences of topological spaces.

Corollary A.3
.

If
P
is a non-discrete poset, then the model structure on
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
transported from
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
along the adjunction
{}{}|-|_{P}\colon\mathrm{sSet}_{P}\leftrightarrow\mathrm{Top}_{P}\colon\mathrm{Sing}_{P}
does not exist.

Let us first deduce the corollary from the proposition.

Proof.

Assume that the transported model structure on
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
exists. Then
{}{}|-|_{P}\dashv\mathrm{Sing}_{P}
is a Quillen adjunction and so the functor
{}{}|-|_{P}
must preserve cofibrations, which implies that the model structure satisfies (
1
). Furthermore,
\mathrm{Sing}_{P}
preserves stratified homotopy equivalences, so the model structure must satisfy (
2
). Finally, if
f\colon X\to Y
is a weak-equivalence, then, by definition,
\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(f)
must be a weak-equivalence in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
. By
Corollary

6.2
, this implies that
f
induces weak-equivalences
\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(X))\to\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(Y))
for all regular flags
\mathcal{I}
. By
Remark

2.33
, this implies that
f
induces weak-equivalences
\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(X)\to\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(Y)
for all regular flags
\mathcal{I}
. In particular, the model category must also satisfy (
3
). But then, it satisfies simultaneously (
1
), (
2
) and (
3
), which is a contradiction.
∎

Proposition

A.1
also has implications for other model structures.

Remark A.4
.

Consider Haine’s model stucture on the category of stratified simplicial sets,
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}^{\text{Joyal-Kan}}

[
Hai18
]
. It can not be transported along the adjunction
({}{}|-|_{P},\mathrm{Sing}_{P})
to a model structure on
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
. Indeed, supposing this is possible, this hypothetical model structure would satisfy (
1
), since the cofibrations in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}^{\text{Joyal-Kan}}
are the monomorphisms. Furthermore, it would satisfy (
2
), since
\mathrm{Sing}_{P}
preserves stratified homotopy equivalences, and those are weak-equivalences in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}^{\text{Joyal-Kan}}
. But then, it can not satisfy (
3
), by
Proposition

A.1
. This implies that if such a model structure existed, the classical homotopy links would not be invariants of the stratified homotopy type.

However, we can show that
some
homotopy links would have to be preserved, preventing the existence of this model structure altogether, by the strengthened version of
Proposition

A.1
(see
Remark

A.2
).
Indeed, let us first assume that
P
admits a successor pair, that is
p<q\in P
such that there exists no
m\in P
satisfying
p<m<q
. Then, by
Lemma

A.8
, and recalling that

\mathrm{Sing}(\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(X))=\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(X)),

we see that
\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}
sends weak equivalences to weak equivalences of spaces, for
\mathcal{I}=[p<q]
. The argument is slightly more subtle if
P
does not admit successor pairs. Let
p<q\in P
, and consider stratified spaces of the form
X\to P
such that the
m
stratum of
X
is empty for all
p<m<q
. Then,
\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}
sends weak equivalences between such stratified spaces to weak equivalences of topological spaces, for
\mathcal{I}=[p<q]
. But this also leads to a contradiction when considering
Example

A.6
as stratified over
\{p<q\}\subset P
.

Remark A.5
.

Similarly, let
\mathrm{Strat}^{N}
be the category of such stratified spaces, which have no empty strata, as defined in
[
NL19
]
. In
[
NL19
]
the author asked the question, whether the structure on the model category for quasi categories
\mathrm{sSet}^{Joyal}
can be transferred to
\mathrm{Strat}^{N}
along an adjunction defined using the functors
({}|-|,\mathrm{Sing}_{\mathrm{Strat}})
. Arguing as in the proof of
Proposition

A.1
, and using
Example

A.6
, we may show that it is indeed not possible.

Assume that the transported model structure exists. Then, since cofibrations in
\mathrm{sSet}^{\text{Joyal}}
are monomorphisms, the model category would satisfy the analogue of (
1
). Furthermore, assume that
H\colon X\times[0,1]\to Y
is a stratified homotopy, between two stratified maps
f,g\colon X\to Y
, and consider the following composition

(16)

\mathrm{Sing}_{\text{Strat}}(X)\times\mathrm{Sing}([0,1])\to\mathrm{Sing}_{\text{Strat}}(X\times[0,1]))\to\mathrm{Sing}_{\text{Strat}}(Y).

This composition is a homotopy (in the Joyal model structure) between
\mathrm{Sing}_{\text{Strat}}(f)
and
\mathrm{Sing}_{\text{Strat}}(g)
. This follows from the fact that
\mathrm{Sing}([0,1])
is a cylinder in the Joyal model structure. In particular, in the transported model structure on
\mathrm{Strat}^{N}
, stratum preserving homotopy equivalences are weak-equivalences, i.e. the model category satisfies the analoge of (
2
).
Now, assume that
f\colon X\to Y
, is a stratum preserving map over the poset
P=\{0<1\}
such that
\mathrm{Sing}_{\text{Strat}}(f)
is a Joyal equivalence in
\mathrm{sSet}
. Then, the map
\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(f)\colon\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(X)\to\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(Y)
is a map in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
, and is a weak equivalence in the structure
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}^{\text{Joyal}}
(that is, the slice model category). But since
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}^{\text{Joyal-Kan}}
is a Bousfield localization of the former,
\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(f)
is also a weak equivalence in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}^{\text{Joyal-Kan}}
. Finally, note that for
P=\{0<1\}
, the model structure on
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}^{\text{Joyal-Kan}}
coincides with the one studied in this paper, giving that
\mathrm{Sing}_{P}(f)
must be a weak equivalence in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
, and hence, must induce weak-equivalences

\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(X)\to\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(Y).

Applying the above to the map of
Example

A.6
gives a contradiction, hence the transported model structure on
\mathrm{Strat}^{N}
may not exist.

Proposition

A.1
is a direct consequence of the following example.

Example A.6
.

Consider the simplicial complex
Y
with vertices
a,b,c,d,e,f
generated by the simplices
\{a,b,c,d\}
,
\{a,c,e\}
and
\{a,d,f\}
and with the stratification over
P=\{0<1\}
defined as follows. The entire segment
\{a,b\}
is sent to
0
, then, consider two segments between
a
and the middle of
\{d,f\}
and between
a
and the middle of
\{e,c\}
, which we will promptly identify with two copies of the interval
[0,1]
(with
a
at
0
). In those intervals, send all the points of the sequence
\frac{1}{2^{n+1}}
, for
n\geq 0
, to
0
(any decreasing sequence converging to
0
will give an isomorphic result). The remaining points are mapped to
1
.

Note that the stratified space obtained in this way is not isomorphic in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
to the realization of a stratified simplicial set. In particular,
Y
does not come from a strongly stratified space. Nevertheless one can compute its homotopy link with respect to the flag
\mathcal{I}=[0<1]
. We will also consider the subspace
X\subset Y
obtained by deleting the maximal simplex
\{a,b,c,d\}
and its face
\{a,c,d\}
, see
Fig.

9
.

We will only be interested in
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(X))
, and
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(Y))
or in other words, in exit-paths up to stratum preserving homotopies. Note that any exit path starting from one of the isolated points is entirely determined - up to stratum preserving homotopy - by its starting point. Similarly, any point starting in the interval
(a,b]
is equivalent to the exit-path spanning the segment
[b,d]
.

On the other hand, there are a vast number of inequivalent classes of exit-paths starting from
a
. A particular set of exit-paths that can be easily described is those that get away from
a
steadily (as in
Fig.

9
). For such an exit-path in
X
, it is enough to specify which face (
\{a,d,f\}
or
\{a,c,e\}
) it lies on, and then for each isolated singular point if the path passes under, or over. This can be summarized as a binary sequence (where the first few terms, corresponding to points away from
a
might be ill-defined). One then checks that two such exit-paths in
X
are equivalent if and only if they lie on the same face and their associated sequence differ in only finitely many places. This implies in particular that
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(X))
is uncountable.

Now consider
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(Y))
. The paths we just described in
X
also exist as exit-paths in
Y
, but there also exist paths going back on forth between the faces
\{a,c,e\}
and
\{a,d,f\}
. If one restricts to those paths that are moving away from
a
, it is still possible to parametrize those by ternary sequences, where the
n
-th entry indicates if the path passes to the left, right or middle of the
n
-th pair of isolated singular points. One can show again that two such paths are equivalent up to stratum-preserving homotopies if and only if their associated sequences differ only at finitely many places. But now, note that an exit-path in
Y
zig-zaging infinitely many times between the left side of
\{a,c,e\}
and the right side of
\{a,d,f\}
can not be in the image of
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(X))\to\pi_{0}(\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(Y))
. This implies that
\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(X)\to\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(Y)
is not a weak-equivalence, and in turn that
X\to Y
is not a weak-equivalence in
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
.

b
c
d
e
f

Figure 9.
the simplicial complexes
X
and
Y
with their pathological stratifications over
P=\{0<1\}
. The isolated singular points admit a as an accumulation point. Two exit-paths starting from
a
have been represented in blue.

Proof of
Proposition

A.1
.

Assume that there exists a model structure on
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
satisfying (
1
) and (
2
). This implies that
{}{}|\Lambda^{\mathcal{J}}_{k}|_{P}\to{}{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}|_{P}
is a trivial cofibration in this model structure for any admissible horn inclusion
\Lambda^{\mathcal{J}}_{k}\to\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}
since it is simultaneously the realization of a cofibration and a stratified homotopy equivalence (see
[
Dou21c
, Proposition 1.13]
). Now note that the inclusion
X\hookrightarrow Y
from
Example

A.6
can be obtained as the following pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\Lambda^{\mathcal{J}}_{k}|_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}|_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}

where
\Lambda^{\mathcal{J}}_{k}\to\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}
is an admissible horn inclusion. In particular,
X\to Y
is the pushout of a trivial cofibration, so it must be a trivial cofibration. But as demonstrated in
Example

A.6
, the inclusion
X\to Y
does not induce weak-equivalences on all classical homotopy links. In particular, the model structure can not satisfy (
3
), which concludes the proof.
∎

Remark A.7
.

One might object, that
Example

A.6
is pathological insofar, as the
0
-stratum considered as a subspace in the usual subspace topology is not
\Delta
-generated. It may be possible to pass to an appropriate tamer subcategory of
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
, which would then allow for the structure of a model category fulfilling all of the requirements of
Proposition

A.1
. The main point of this article however, is that even without attempting to change the underlying point-set topological framework and defining such a model structure, many of the consequences of its existence may nevertheless be obtained.

Lemma A.8
.

Let
\mathcal{I}=\{p<q\}
be a flag in
P
and let
X\in\mathrm{sSet}_{P}^{Joyal-Kan}
be such that
X_{m}
is empty, for all
p<m<q
. Then, for any weak equivalence
X\to Y
in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}^{Joyal-Kan}
, the induced map on simplicial homotopy links,
\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(X)\to\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(Y)
, is a weak equivalence in the Kan model structure on simplicial sets.

Proof.

Recall that the fibrant objects in
\mathrm{sSet}^{\mathrm{Joyal-Kan}}_{P}
are those that admit the right lifting property against all stratified horn inclusions
\Lambda^{\mathcal{J}}_{k}
which are either inner horn inclusions, or admissible horn inclusions. Call those horn inclusions weakly admissible. Now, given a weak-equivalence in
\mathrm{sSet}^{\mathrm{Joyal-Kan}}_{P}
,
f\colon X\to Y
, where the
m
-stratum of
X
is empty for all
p<m<q
, consider fibrant replacements for
X
and
Y
obtained by the small object argument applied to the set of weakly admissible horn inclusions. We get a commutative diagram of weak equivalences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\text{fib}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\text{fib}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\text{fib}}}

Now, since
\mathrm{sSet}^{\mathrm{Joyal-Kan}}_{P}
is a left Bousfield localization of
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
(see
Remark

2.55
), and since the map
f^{\text{fib}}
is a weak equivalence between fibrant object in
\mathrm{sSet}^{\mathrm{Joyal-Kan}}_{P}
, it must also be a weak equivalence in
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
. But then, by
Corollary

6.2
,
f^{\text{fib}}
must induce a weak equivalence
\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(X^{\text{fib}})\to\mathrm{HoLink}_{\mathcal{I}}(Y^{\text{fib}})
. By two out of three, it is thus enough to show
Lemma

A.8
for maps of the form
X\hookrightarrow X^{\text{fib}}
(noting that the
m
-strata of
Y
must also be empty for
p<m<q
, since weak equivalences in
\mathrm{sSet}^{\mathrm{Joyal-Kan}}_{P}
preserve the homotopy type of strata).
Now, by
Theorem

1.4
, together with the (pseudo)-naturality of
\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}
with respect to monomorphisms (see
Proposition

4.1
), we can reduce to comparing
\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(X)
and
\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(X^{\mathrm{fib}})
. Finally, since the map
X\hookrightarrow X^{\text{fib}}

is a transfinite composition of pushouts along weakly admissible horn inclusions, and since
\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}
preserves colimits, it is enough to prove
Lemma

A.8

for maps
X\hookrightarrow Y
obtained by pushing out along a single weakly admissible horn inclusion,
\Lambda^{\mathcal{J}}_{k}\to\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}
. Now, note that since
X
has empty
m
strata for all
p<m<q
, so must have
\Lambda^{\mathcal{J}}_{k}
, and
\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}
. Finally, it is enough to show that

\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(\Lambda^{\mathcal{J}}_{k})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(\Delta^{\mathcal{J}})

is a weak equivalence of simplicial sets, whenever
\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}
has empty
m
-strata, for
p<m<q
.
Let
\mathcal{J}=[p_{0}\leq\dots\leq p_{n}]
, and consider the following cases.

•

if
\Lambda^{\mathcal{J}}_{k}
is an admissible horn, then this follows from
Lemma

4.7
.

•

else,
\Lambda^{\mathcal{J}}_{k}
is an inner horn, and thus
n\geq 2
. But then, the only vertices that can possibly be in
\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(\Delta^{\mathcal{J}})
but not in
\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(\Lambda^{\mathcal{J}}_{k})
are those corresponding to the simplex
\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}

and to its face,
\Delta^{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}=d_{k}(\Delta^{\mathcal{J}})
. Those would be in
\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(\Delta^{\mathcal{J}})
if and only if they degenerate from
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
. If the former degenerates from
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
,
\Lambda^{\mathcal{J}}_{k}
is an admissible horn. If the latter degenerates from
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
,
then
\Delta^{\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}=[p_{0},\dots,\widehat{p_{k}},\dots,p_{n}]
with
p_{i}=p
or
q
for all
i\not=k
. But since
p_{k-1}\leq p_{k}\leq p_{k+1}
,
and since
\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}
has empty
m
-strata for all
p<m<q
, then
p_{k}=p
or
q
, and the horn is also admissible. Finally, in all the remaining cases, we have
\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(\Lambda^{\mathcal{J}}_{k})=\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(\Delta^{\mathcal{J}})
.

∎

Appendix B
Relating labellings, vertical stratifications and diagrams.

In
Remark

6.16
we have already hinted at the fact that

P
-labelled simplicial sets can be thought of as a particularly concise description of certain cofibrant diagrams in
\mathrm{Diag}_{P}
. Let us now expand on this and make the relationship between labelled objects, diagrams and vertical objects precise.
Before we do so, let us quickly remark on the topological counterpart of
\mathrm{Diag}_{P}
. We denote
\mathrm{Diag}_{P}^{\mathrm{Top}}:=\mathrm{Fun}(R(P)^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Top})
.

Remark B.1
.

Just like its simplicial counterpart
\mathrm{Diag}_{P}^{\mathrm{Top}}
can be equipped with the projective model structure (use for example
[
Hir09
, Thrm 11.6.1]
). Assigning to a
P
-stratified space
X
the diagram given by its (topological) homotopy links
\mathcal{I}\mapsto\mathcal{H}\!\textit{o}\mathrm{Link}_{\mathcal{I}}(X)
, and conversely sending a diagram
F
to
\int^{\mathcal{I}}F(\mathcal{I})\times{}{}|\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}|_{P}
then defines a Quillen adjunction

D^{\prime}_{P}\colon\mathrm{Diag}_{P}^{\mathrm{Top}}\leftrightarrow\mathrm{Top}_{P}\colon C^{\prime}_{P}.

We obtain a diagram of Quillen functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Diag}^{\mathrm{Top}}_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Diag}_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Top}_{P},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\prime}_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\mathrm{Top}}_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{\mathrm{Top}}_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{\prime}_{P}}

where the left diagonals are induced by the adjunction
{}|-|\dashv\mathrm{Sing}
. Both the left adjoint, as well as the right adjoint part of this diagram commute up to natural isomorphism. Hence, it follows from the two out of three property for Quillen equivalences that
C^{\prime}_{P}\dashv D^{\prime}_{P}
is also a Quillen equivalence.

Next, let us describe the relationship between labelled objects and diagrams defined on
R(P)^{op}
.
Both
P
-labeled simplicial sets, as well as CW-complexes can readily be equipped with a functor

\displaystyle U\colon P\text{-}\mathrm{sSet}

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{Diag}_{P},

\displaystyle U^{\mathrm{Top}}\colon P\text{-}\mathrm{CW}

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{Diag}_{P}^{\mathrm{Top}}

respectively.
In case of a
P
-labeled CW-complex
(T,\lambda_{T})
, the diagram
U^{\mathrm{Top}}(T,\lambda_{T})\in\mathrm{Diag}_{P}^{\mathrm{Top}}
, at a regular flag
\mathcal{I}
, is given by the unions of cells

U^{\mathrm{Top}}(T,\lambda_{T})(\mathcal{I})=\bigcup_{e_{\alpha},\ {\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}}\subset\lambda_{T}(e_{\alpha})}e_{\alpha},

and structure maps are given by inclusions. One extends this construction to morphisms in the obvious way.
The definition for simplicial sets is analogous, replacing open cells by non-degenerate simplices. The precise behavior of the
U
functors is then described in the following proposition:

Proposition B.2
.

The functors
U
and
U^{\mathrm{Top}}
are fully faithful, and fit into a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P\text{-}\mathrm{sSet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Diag}_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P\text{-}\mathrm{CW}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Diag}_{P}^{\mathrm{Top}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{\mathrm{Top}}}

with verticals induced by realization.
Both
U
and
U^{\mathrm{Top}}
factor thought the respective subcategories of cofibrant diagrams.
Furthermore,
U
induces an equivalence of categories
P\text{-}\mathrm{sSet}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Diag}_{P}^{\mathrm{cof}}
.

Proof.

Commutativity is easily verified from the constructions, while fully faithfulness is an immediate consequence of the definition of labelled maps. That both
U
functors have image in the subcategories of cofibrant objects follows from the characterization of generating cofibrations in a projective model structure in
[
Hir09
, Thm. 11.6.1]
. Finally, that every cofibrant diagram in
\mathrm{Diag}_{P}
is in fact (up to natural isomorphism) of the form
U(S,\lambda_{S})
, for
(S,\lambda_{S})\in P\text{-}\mathrm{sSet}
, follows from the characterization of cofibrant diagrams in
Proposition

2.42
(see also
Remark

6.16
).
∎

In this sense,
P
-labeled simplicial sets are a particularly concise, but equivalent, description of cofibrant diagrams. Since not every absolute cell complex is a CW-complex, the analogous essential surjectivity fails for
P
-labelled CW-complexes. However, as a consequence of
Theorem

6.26
, essential surjectivity is restored when passing to homotopy categories.

Next, let us study the precise relationship of diagrams with verticalization.
One easily verifies that, the two diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P\text{-}\mathrm{sSet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Diag}_{P}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\,,}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sSet}_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P\text{-}\mathrm{CW}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Diag}^{\mathrm{Top}}_{P}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\,,}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Top}_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{\mathrm{Top}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\prime}_{P}}

commute up to natural isomorphism. In this sense, we can think of verticalization as an alternative description of the
C_{P}
functors. Now, clearly the verticalization functors are not full. We can amend this by passing to the vertical setting. Denote by
\mathrm{V}\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
and
\mathrm{VCW}_{P}
respectively the categories of vertically stratified simplicial sets and CW-spaces, with vertical maps. Using the natural pre-verticalizations which the verticalization functors come with, we obtain lifts

\displaystyle V\colon P\text{-}\mathrm{sSet}

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{V}\mathrm{sSet}_{P}

\displaystyle(S,\lambda_{S})

\displaystyle\to\{V(S,\lambda_{S})\hookrightarrow S\times N(P)\},

\displaystyle V\colon P\text{-}\mathrm{CW}

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{VCW}_{P}

\displaystyle(T,\lambda_{T})

\displaystyle\to\{V(T,\lambda_{T})\hookrightarrow T\times{}{}|N(P)|_{P}\},

denoted the same by abuse of notation. It turns out, that both these functos induce equivalences of categories. One may immediately verify from the definition of label preserving maps, that these two functors are fully faithful. They are essentially surjective, by construction of the categories of vertical objects.

Hence, verticalization defines equivalences between
P
-labeled and vertical objects. In this sense, using
Proposition

B.2
, the vertical categories give a description of (certain) cofibrant diagrams, which is more intrinsic to
\mathrm{sSet}_{P}
or
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
.
Together with
Proposition

B.2
we may summarize the whole situation in the following proposition.

Proposition B.3
.

The following diagram of functors commutes up to natural isomorphism.

(17)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sSet}_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{V}\mathrm{sSet}_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P\text{-}\mathrm{sSet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Diag}_{P}^{\mathrm{cof}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Top}_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{VCW}_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P\text{-}\mathrm{CW}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Diag}_{P}^{\mathrm{Top},\mathrm{cof}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f.f.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{\mathrm{Top}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f.f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\prime}_{P}}

Here, all verticals are induced by realization and the left two horizontals are given by the forgetful functors forgetting the pre-verticalizations. Both
V
functors define equivalences of categories. The
U
functors are both fully faithful, and the upper one is even an equivalence of categories.

The fact that
P
-labeled CW-complexes embed fully faithfully into
\mathrm{Diag}^{\mathrm{Top},\mathrm{cof}}_{P}
(
Proposition

B.2
), allows one to model the homotopy category
\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Top}_{P}
through these objects. To see this, we first need a definition of label preserving homotopy.

Definition B.4
.

Let
f,g\colon(T,\lambda_{T})\to(T^{\prime},\lambda_{T^{\prime}})

be two label preserving maps between
P
-labelled CW-complexes. A
label preserving homotopy
from
f
to
g
is a map,

H\colon T\times[0,1]\to T^{\prime},

such that for all cells
e_{\alpha}\in T
and
e_{\beta}\in T^{\prime}
, if
H(e_{\alpha}\times[0,1])\cap e_{\beta}\not=\emptyset
, then
\lambda_{T}(e_{\alpha})\subset\lambda_{T^{\prime}}(e_{\beta})
. Equivalently,
H
is a label-preserving map for the induced labelling on
T\times[0,1]
. If such a homotopy exists, we say
f
and
g
are label preserving homotopic, writing
f\simeq_{\text{lab}}g
.
We write
P\text{-}\mathrm{CW}/{\simeq_{\text{lab}}}
for the category whose objects are
P
-labelled CW-complex and whose sets of morphisms between
(T,\lambda_{T})
and
(T^{\prime},\lambda_{T^{\prime}})
is
P\text{-}\mathrm{CW}((T,\lambda_{T}),(T^{\prime},\lambda_{T^{\prime}}))/{\simeq_{\text{lab}}}
.

Remark B.5
.

Note, that under the fully faithful functor
V\colon P\text{-}\mathrm{CW}\to\mathrm{VCW}_{P}
,
P
-labelled homotopies correspond one to one to vertical homotopies. Furthermore, under the fully faithful functor
U^{\mathrm{Top}}\colon P\text{-}\mathrm{CW}\to\mathrm{Diag}^{\mathrm{Top},\mathrm{cof}}
they correspond one to one to those homotopies in
\mathrm{Diag}_{P}^{\mathrm{Top}}
which are defined through the cylinder given by
(F\times[0,1])(\mathcal{I})=F(\mathcal{I})\times[0,1]
.

We may use
Proposition

B.3
to obtain a more high-level proof of part of
Proposition

6.24
. We focus on the
\mathrm{Top}_{P}
part here. A proof for
\mathrm{Top}_{N(P)}
works analogously.

Alternative proof of
Proposition

6.24
.

Let us first focus on the absolute case.
By
Remark

B.5
and
Proposition

B.3
, we may equivalently show that in the following following commutative diagram of categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P\text{-}\mathrm{CW}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Diag}_{P}^{\mathrm{Top},\mathrm{cof}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Top}^{\mathrm{cof}}_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P\text{-}\mathrm{CW}/{\simeq_{\text{lab}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Diag}_{P}^{\mathrm{Top},\mathrm{cof}}/_{\sim}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Top}_{P},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{\mathrm{Top}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f.f.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\prime}_{P}}

the lower horizontal composition is fully faithful. Here, by
\mathrm{Diag}_{P}^{\mathrm{Top},\mathrm{cof}}/_{\sim}
we denote the category obtained by identifying morphisms which are homotopic through the cylinder given by
(F\times[0,1])(\mathcal{I})=F(\mathcal{I})\times[0,1]
. By
Proposition

B.3
, the left upper horizontal is fully faithful. Using the second part of
Remark

B.5
, we also obtain that the left lower horizontal is fully faithful.
Since every object in
\mathrm{Diag}^{\mathrm{Top}}_{P}
is fibrant, we have a natural equivalence
\mathrm{Diag}_{P}^{\mathrm{Top},\mathrm{cof}}/_{\sim}\cong\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Diag}^{\mathrm{Top}}_{P}
. Now, finally note that
C^{\prime\mathrm{Top}}_{P}\colon\mathrm{Diag}^{\mathrm{Top}}_{P}\to\mathrm{Top}_{P}
is the left part of a Quillen equivalence (see
Remark

B.1
). It follows, that the last remaining lower horizontal arrow is also fully faithful, finishing the proof of the absolute case. The relative case follows analogously, after verifying that
U^{\mathrm{Top}}
maps inclusion
(A,\lambda_{A})\hookrightarrow(T,\lambda_{T})
(where
\lambda_{A}
is the induced labelling) to a cofibration in the projective model structure on
\mathrm{Diag}_{P}^{\mathrm{Top}}
.
∎

Finally, we may also use
Proposition

B.3
together with
Remark

B.5
to obtain the following alternative version of
Theorem

6.26
, which allows one to perform stratified homotopy theory in the labelled setting.

Corollary B.6
.

Verticalization induces an equivalence of categories

P\text{-}\mathrm{CW}/{\simeq_{\textnormal{lab}}}\cong\mathrm{Ho}\mathrm{Top}_{P}.
